# Token-Generation Improvement Round — Task Tracker

Goal (from `do_your_best.md`): best possible generation quality (no regression), faster prefill/prompt caching, and above all faster token generation on 2x (future 4x) Tesla V100. Baselines and setup details: see memory `v100-inference-setup.md` and commit `d2b706d1b`.

Status legend: ✅ done · 🔄 in progress · ⬜ pending

## ⬜ Pending (approved roadmap, not started)

- [x] **9. GPU sampling in tensor-split mode** — investigated and measured, **not implemented (no headroom)**. Two findings: (a) the server disables backend sampling whenever speculation is active (`tools/server/server-context.cpp:1848`), so it can never help the MTP prod config; (b) for nospec serving the entire CPU sampling path is free — 4-conc aggregate 89.6 t/s greedy vs 89.5 t/s with full top-k/top-p/min-p chain vs 89.7 control (within noise): logits transfer + CPU sampler fully overlap GPU compute. Implementing would require a new allgather subgraph-boundary type in the meta backend (vocab-split logits hit the `handle_per_row` assert) for a measured ~0% gain.
- [x] **10. KV cache compression (TurboQuant)** — resolved: **existing quantized KV already meets the goal; TurboQuant not worth implementing.** Findings (Qwen3.6-27B, tensor mode, wikitext-2):
  - Quantized KV **works in tensor-split mode** (old note saying otherwise was stale), and this tree already ships upstream PR #21038 (commit `744c0c731`): Hadamard-rotated KV quantization — the same core trick as TurboQuant/QuaRot — auto-enabled whenever KV is quantized (opt-out `LLAMA_ATTN_ROT_DISABLE=1`).
  - PPL @32k ctx: f16 6.2904 · q8_0/q8_0 6.2922 (+0.03%, noise, **lossless, 2×**) · K q8_0/V q4_0 6.2993 (+0.14%, **2.67×**) · q4_0/q4_0 6.3093 (+0.30%, **4×**). @8k ctx all statistically identical. temp-0 128-tok gen byte-identical to f16 for q8_0 and q4_0. tg cost ≈ −3-4%.
  - Mixed K/V types crashed in tensor mode: default builds compile no FA kernel for K≠V (`fattn.cu:424`), the scheduler tries CPU fallback and the meta backend asserts (`ggml-backend-meta.cpp:557`). Fixed by building with `GGML_CUDA_FA_ALL_QUANTS=ON` (added to `.devops/cuda.Dockerfile`). Known residual: unsupported-op CPU fallback in tensor mode aborts instead of erroring cleanly.
  - TurboQuant itself (arXiv 2504.19874): no official code; needs a non-block ggml type (Lloyd-Max codebooks + per-token norms + QJL residual) and codebook gathers inside the V100 FA vec kernels; would buy ~3.5 effective bits vs q4_0+rotation's 4.5 (≈22% more capacity) — 2026 practice (SAW-INT4, vLLM) converged on rotated token-wise INT4, which is exactly what's already in-tree.
  - **Recommended prod setting: `-ctk q8_0 -ctv q8_0`** (strictly lossless, halves KV → doubles usable context/slots); `-ctk q8_0 -ctv q4_0` if more capacity is worth +0.14% ppl.
- [x] **11. Close the remaining 4-concurrency gap** — ✅ done (`abf23cdd1` + `65286b53f`). The open temp-0 drift was resolved by the simplify pass: the duplicate per-entry `graph_params` construction in `process_ubatch` was the culprit; with params built once and only the `res` pointer patched, fresh-server and cached-prompt np1 outputs both match the pre-split baseline byte-for-byte, and 4-conc spec holds at 125.8 t/s. Root cause: hybrid models (Qwen3.6 = attention + recurrent DeltaNet) force per-seq ubatch splits when spec rollback is on (`[TAG_RECURRENT_ROLLBACK_SPLITS]`), so the 4-slot verify ran as 4×35ms single-seq decodes instead of one 16-token ubatch (74.5ms; decode-scaling curve: 1 tok=20.8ms, 4=40.4, 8=69.6, 12=65.8 (MMVQ→MMQ crossover at 8), 16=74.5, 32=123).
  Fix: `split_equal(n_ubatch, sequential, full_seqs=true)` in `llama-batch.{h,cpp}` — groups consecutive equal-count seqs whole so no seq spans ubatches (preserves the rollback-snapshot invariant in `delta-net-base.cpp`); used by the rollback branches in `llama-memory-recurrent.cpp`, `llama-memory-hybrid.cpp`, `llama-memory-hybrid-iswa.cpp`.
  **Measured: 4-conc spec aggregate 64.7 → 125.0 t/s (nospec is 89.6 — spec now wins by 40%).** Single-stream unchanged (65.8).
  Before commit: (a) root-cause a 1-token temp-0 drift at np1 on fresh server only (output coherent, deterministic per build, matches baseline exactly when prompt is cached → suspect checkpoint/prefill interaction; diff full `LLAMA_BATCH_DEBUG=2` dumps of fresh 256-token runs on both builds); (b) validate np4 spec temp-0 outputs are coherent vs nospec (multi-seq K-snapshot path in `ggml_gated_delta_net` newly exercised); (c) quick ppl run to confirm no quality regression.
- [x] **13. GLM-4.7-Flash / MLA tensor-mode support** — ✅ **RESOLVED** (gate lifted 2026-07-07; new model **GLM-4.7-Flash-UD** validated 2026-07-09). Tensor mode is **byte-identical / PPL-neutral**; the historical "corrupts after ~2 tokens" was **MoE-router amplification of ordinary split-reduction noise + a tail-reduce bug** (fixed `d534f0aba`), not a split-state bug — see the RESOLVED and long-context sub-bullets below. **Recommendation: run GLM single-GPU** — it's as fast as tensor mode for an A3B MoE, and MLA is so KV-efficient the new GLM-4.7-Flash-UD holds its **full 202K native context on one 32 GB V100** (~10 GB KV), so 2-GPU tensor adds nothing. Historical root cause (v1 design): `deepseek2` arch with MLA (`n_head_kv=1`, one shared 576-dim latent) — the split policy head-splits `cache_k_l*`, colliding with the mirrored latent at `handle_set_rows` (pre-existing crash). Implemented v1 (mirror the whole attention block for `is_mla()` models + mirrored-QKV case in `handle_flash_attn_ext`; MoE experts still split): no longer crashes, 79.8 t/s. Verified inert for non-MLA models (Qwen temp-0 byte-identical).

Key mechanism: sources always resolve with `assume_sync=true` (ggml-backend-meta.cpp ~932), so PARTIAL is visible only at the node itself and correctness rests entirely on AllReduce placement — silent by construction, no assert can fire.
  - **AllReduce placement verified correct for GLM** via new env `GGML_META_DEBUG_REDUCE=1` (prints `partial N -> boundary M`; placement logic is n-independent, so a 1-GPU run is authoritative): boundary lands on the final expert-sum `ffn_moe_out` ADD, BEFORE the `ggml_add(moe_out, ffn_shexp)` merge; the delayed window contains only sum-commuting ops (mul by mirrored weights, expert-view adds). Audit suspects #1 (shexp merged pre-reduce) and #2 (get_i_delayed mismatch on GLM's op order) are **cleared**.

(5) mirrored `cache_k_l*` desync via the zero-slice compute-flag disable inside delayed windows ("corrupts after ~2 tokens" fits cache divergence compounding).
  - **No 2-GPU box needed anymore**: `LLAMA_META_DUP_DEVICE=2` (d534f0aba) duplicates the device list so ONE GPU runs a genuine n=2 split.   - **Fix landed along the way (d534f0aba)**: the AllReduce loop skipped the LAST subgraph of every graph — a graph ending in a PARTIAL node (sched split after a partial, or per-node debug execution) silently never reduced. Subgraphs now carry a planning-time reduce flag. Also: meta get_tensor learned PARTIAL reads (sum of slices), making `eval-callback` usable on meta backends.

The observed temp-0 text divergence is **MoE-router amplification of ordinary split-reduction noise** (near-tied experts flip; dense Qwen shows zero divergence because nothing discontinuous amplifies the noise). **Quality is unaffected: PPL 14.3574±0.69 single-GPU vs 14.3787±0.69 at n=2 (dup harness) — statistically identical.** The historical "corrupts after ~2 tokens" is best explained by the tail-reduce bug fixed in d534f0aba (MoE routing's sched splits can end a graph on an unreduced PARTIAL — real corruption, silent, exactly matched the symptom). **Physical 2-GPU confirmation PASSED** (same day): Qwen 2-GPU byte-identical to reference (gates the meta fixes on real hardware); GLM 2-GPU coherent (30.6 t/s under heavy host-GPU contention — not a perf number). **Gate lifted**: the model-create rejection is now a WARN (attention mirrored, single GPU usually as fast); `LLAMA_TENSOR_MLA_WIP` env removed. Tensor mode works (2-GPU `-sm tensor -ts 0.5,0.5` byte-identical to single-GPU). **Capacity conclusion — 2-GPU tensor buys NOTHING for this model**: MLA KV is only **~10.2 GB for the *full* 202,752-token native context** (measured VRAM delta: `-c 4096` → 17.2 GB peak, `-c 202752` → 27.6 GB peak, both 1 GPU), so **one 32 GB V100 holds GLM's entire native context with ~5 GB headroom** at ~89 t/s. Measured ceilings (1 GPU): `-c 131072` fits (94 t/s), `-c 202752` fits (94 t/s). 2-GPU tensor at full context also fits but is **slower (70 t/s)** — AllReduce overhead, no compute or capacity benefit. **Recommended GLM config = single-GPU, `-c 202752`** (or any context up to native). Tensor mode only helps a model whose weights+KV exceed one card; MLA makes GLM not that model. Optional: q8_0 KV would halve the ~10 GB KV → ~22 GB total at full context, more headroom still.
- [x] **14. Meta-device model teardown segfault** — ✅ resolved (2026-07-07), and the original diagnosis was wrong: `llama_model_free` on a meta-device model without a context is SAFE (verified under gdb with a breakpoint on the free — clean exit; likely fixed incidentally by the shadow/fingerprint refactors, or never broken). The actual segfault behind "context-creation failure crashes" was the **server dereferencing the null context** (`llama_n_ctx(nullptr)` in `load_model` — reproduced with `-sm tensor -fa off`, backtrace lands in load_model, not in any free). Fixes: (a) server checks `ctx == nullptr` after init; (b) systemic: `common_init_from_params` now DROPS the model when context creation (or lora load) fails, so the four other tools that only check `model()==nullptr` (results, cvector-generator, mtmd-cli, perplexity) are covered without touching them; (c) bonus: fixed a pre-existing **double-free** in the lora-failure path (`pimpl->model.reset(model)` re-stored the pointer the unique_ptr already owned). Verified: `-fa off` in tensor mode now exits with a clean error; positive path (`-fa on`) byte-identical to reference. The MLA gate stays at model create — erroring before the weights load is better UX anyway. Footnote: teardown verified with a 1-GPU meta device (GPU0 occupied); re-run the `-fa off` repro with both GPUs free for completeness.
- [x] **23. SSD-streaming correctness hardening** — ✅ **DONE (2026-07-10, `37e21180a`) with one recorded residual.** (a) same-pass eviction pinning (pass epochs; overshoot+warn instead of zeroed experts — the MRU-front insight makes the pinned-tail check O(1)); (b) pread failures abort loudly (serial + parallel); (c) eval-callback displacement warns (`llama-context.cpp`); (d) defensive H2D sync before pins release. **Gates: PPL byte-equal at 24 GB / 1 GB / 512 MB budgets (7.0898 / 6.2022 / 5.1397); overshoot warning fires at 48 MB.** **RESIDUAL (open):** at ~48 MB — less than ONE node's expert union, ~100× below practical budgets — output is deterministic but slightly divergent (chunk-1 PPL 4.0573 vs 4.0331). NOT a race (identical across two builds, sync made no difference; my async-DMA hypothesis disproven) — a systematic path difference at absurd budgets, unisolated. Practical impact nil; revisit if tiny-budget configs ever matter. Original finding text: Three verified silent-corruption hazards, none yet observed in practice but all reachable: (a) **same-pass eviction** — within one `gpu_bind`/prefill pass, a slice that was a RAM *hit* early in the loop can be evicted (`MADV_DONTNEED`) by a later insertion in the same pass when the node's expert working set exceeds `LLAMA_SSD_STREAM_BUDGET`; phase-3 H2D (or `copy_experts`) then reads zeroed pages — **silent garbage** instead of the documented "budget must cover one ubatch's union" caveat failing loudly. Fix: pin this-pass slices against eviction (or force-read every VRAM miss in phase 2); minimum: detect + abort. (b) **pread failures swallowed** — `ssd_pread_full`'s bool is ignored and the parallel workers discard returns; a slice is inserted as resident *before* its deferred read runs, so a transient disk error poisons the cache permanently. Fix: propagate failure, evict the slice from the index, abort loudly. (c) **eval-callback conflict** (`llama-context.cpp:1432`) — the sched has ONE eval-cb slot; any `cb_eval` (e.g. the `LLAMA_DEBUG` dump cb) displaces the SSD fill cb → streamed CPU-path experts never fill → silent garbage. Fix: warn at minimum; ideally chain (wrapper cb runs the fill then forwards).
- [x] **24. SSD-streaming lifecycle + robustness** — ✅ **DONE (2026-07-10) except (d), deliberately deferred.** (a) `ssd_stream_reset()` — full teardown (VRAM pools, ids scratches, dup fds, registry, SLRU) hooked into the LAST arena buffer's free (refcounted; fires on `llama_model_free`, i.e. the server model-swap path that used to leak VRAM+fds and risk stale-madvise into a reused arena). Note: cli/perplexity leak-at-exit by design and never free model buffers, so the reset doesn't log there — verified the code ships (string in libggml-base) and the mechanism is the buffer-free path itself. (b) **pool-OOM halving fallback**: `ensure_pool` halves K on alloc failure instead of leaving bind to first-token-abort — gate: `VRAM_BUDGET=40960` on a 32 GB card = 3 halvings, runs at 5.4 t/s, zero aborts (was: death). (c) **backend-type gate**: shrink + ssd_gpu now also require the split backend to BE a GPU device (no more "VRAM pool" on the CPU backend when op_offload is off). Regression: PPL 7.0898 unchanged. **(d) mutex narrowing — DEFERRED**: releasing `g_state.mutex` across phase-2/3 interacts with the pass-pinning epochs (another context's pass increment would unpin in-flight slices → reintroduces the #23 corruption); needs per-pass pin ownership, only matters for multi-model-per-process serving. Original finding text: (a) **No teardown**: `g_gpu_pools` (VRAM buffers), `fd_dup`, registry, and the SLRU state are process-global and never reset — server model-swap leaks VRAM+fds, and the stale SLRU can `madvise(MADV_DONTNEED)` into a *reused* arena address range of the NEXT model (cross-model corruption). Add `ggml_ssd_stream_reset()` called from model unload. (b) **First-token `GGML_ABORT` on pool OOM**: the reclaim shrinks the copy at reserve time, so a too-big `VRAM_BUDGET` kills the process at the first decode token instead of degrading — un-shrink + CPU-fallback retry, or validate the pool before committing the shrink. (c) **Gate on the actual backend type**: shrink/bind gate on `n_gpu==1` but never check the split backend IS the GPU — with op_offload off, a CPU-scheduled streamed matmul gets a "VRAM pool" allocated on the CPU backend (wrong tier, silent). (d) **Mutex scope**: `g_state.mutex` is held across the phase-2 disk reads and phase-3 H2D issue — two models in one process serialize on each other's I/O; shrink the critical section to the SLRU/pool bookkeeping.

- [x] **26. Distributed: worker-local model sourcing (skip the first-load network stream)** — ✅ **DONE (2026-07-10); real-fleet timing measurement pending.** Implemented exactly as designed, no protocol change: `rpc-server -md/--model-dir <dir>` — `rpc_server::build_local_index()` walks the dir recursively (matches `.gguf` anywhere in the name — the `?download=true` files count), parses each GGUF via the in-tree gguf API, and fnv-hashes every tensor **> HASH_THRESHOLD (10 MiB)** — only those go through the coordinator's `SET_TENSOR_HASH` path — into `hash → (file, offset, size)`. Index persists as a text sidecar in the cache dir (`modelidx-<fnv(path|mtime|size)>`), so restarts load instantly; no cache dir = re-hash each start (warned). Lookup: `set_tensor_hash` tries the weight cache, then `get_local_tensor()` — pread from the GGUF + **re-hash verification before serving** (a file changed after indexing falls back to streaming loudly). Coordinator untouched and unaware. Verified (loopback, CPU build): 0.6B — 311 MB embedding served from the local GGUF on a cold cache, generation byte-identical to a no-RPC reference; persisted index reloads instantly ("from cached index"); **mismatch gate**: worker indexed the 9B while the coordinator loaded the 0.6B → 0 local serves, clean streaming fallback, correct output. Known scope limits: (a) tensor-parallel islands stream slices, which never hash-match whole tensors — slice-aware indexing would be a follow-up; (b) tensors ≤ 10 MiB always stream (coordinator never hashes them). **Real-fleet validation DONE (2026-07-10 late evening):** all four workers ran `--model-dir` with the 35B GGUF placed locally (compose hook `WORKER_MODELS_DIR`/`WORKER_EXTRA_ARGS`, commit `101026ed5`); a **topology-cold** 4-worker load (fresh slice assignment: Ultra9 11.5 GB / i7-8550U 5.7 / i5 3.0 / 1660 Ti 1.0) completed in **~4 min vs ~24 min** for the earlier pure-stream cold load to the 100-Mbit-class boxes — **~6× when it matters most**, and topology-proof where the `-c` cache is topology-sensitive (fleet churn re-deals slices every load). Not the pure-local floor because ≤10 MiB tensors always stream, hash round-trips are per-tensor, and the slow boxes also have slow disks. Fleet link reality check recorded: `.15` does ~130 MB/s (GbE); the i7-8550U/i5 boxes only ~15 MB/s (100-Mbit-class links) — which makes local sourcing disproportionately valuable there.

Loopback approximation: 2 workers + `GGML_RPC_DEBUG_FAIL_ALLOC` injection on one, watch whether the OTHER worker survives the fused-boundary path.

The failing run was `run-fleet-deepseek-autoweight.sh` at 2026-07-15 20:00:40Z (`-sm layer` + `--rpc-discover`, fleet-coordinator compose - no `--rpc-reload`), NOT the EP script (its edited runs were 17:08-17:12Z and their logs are gone); the user had started a local CPU worker on the coordinator box (:50053, `--announce`) at 19:40Z to add this box's CPU to the discovered fleet. Timeline: coordinator up 20:00:43Z (first conn on the :50053 worker) -> .11 starts failing W2W pulls 20:05:12Z -> .11 worker dies 20:14:16Z (docker restarts it) -> load/serve churn until teardown ~20:47Z.
  - **(a) ANSWERED - the 6.5 GiB was the 1660 Ti's LAYER-SPLIT weight share, not an EP/tensor-mode buffer.** Auto-weight bailed (.15 was still on the 4.6 image = unscored; auto-weight requires every worker scored) -> memory-proportional DEFAULT split, which has NO capacity cap: when the model (80.75 GiB weights) exceeds the fleet's summed free memory, every device's share exceeds its free memory by construction (share_i = free_i/sum_free x model). ~5.9 GiB free of a ~74 GiB fleet sum = 6.4 GiB share on a 6144 MiB card -> cudaMalloc refused. For the record, the EP config CANNOT produce this: measured from the v2 GGUF, EP_ONLY mirrors only 2806 MiB onto every member (74304 MiB `_exps` split by -ts, 5579 MiB DSA attention dedicated to the owner), so a zero-share 1660 Ti member carries ~2.8 GiB and fits. FIX: capacity-check the default split (clean per-device "share X MiB > device free Y MiB" error at load, or cap+spill like auto-weight).
  - **(b) ANSWERED - containment works as designed, no fix needed.** `rpc_server::alloc_buffer` returns remote_ptr 0 on a failed alloc and the worker stays up (ggml-rpc.cpp:2555; upstream CUDA alloc returns nullptr with exactly the logged error, no abort); the meta/client side frees partial member allocs and fails the load cleanly. .15's worker was never lost by the coordinator.
  - **(c) ANSWERED - .11 genuinely died, killed by a W2W endpoint-relativity bug amplified by an fd leak.** (1) The coordinator heard the local worker's multicast beacon via LOOPBACK (same-host datagram) and registered it as `127.0.0.1:50053`; (2) every W2W handoff with that worker as source shipped the literal endpoint string to .11 (`COPY_FROM_REMOTE`), which dialed ITSELF -> ECONNREFUSED -> soft-fallback to the bridged copy (correctness preserved; 1050 `[copy_from_remote] cannot reach source worker '127.0.0.1:50053'` lines in 9 min - no negative cache, it re-dials every handoff); (3) `socket_t::connect` LEAKS the fd on every failure path (transport.cpp:657 `::connect` fail, plus the set_no_delay and gethostbyname branches; `create_server`'s bind/listen fails leak too) - the fd is only handed to the RAII holder on success; at the container's 1024 soft nofile limit `accept()` failed; (4) the serve loop treats ANY accept failure as FATAL (ggml-rpc.cpp:4118 `return`) -> worker exit 20:14:16Z -> `restart: always` brought it back -> the coordinator's next command on the dead cached socket (get_device_memory, fleet status poll) printed "connection lost or worker crashed". FIX LIST: (1) ✅ close the fd on all `socket_t::connect`/`create_server`/`accept` failure paths [also an upstream bug]; (2) ✅ accept() failure: log + retry with 200ms sleep (cap 300 consecutive = ~60s, then exit for the supervisor - a wedged listener still recovers via restart policy), never die on one EMFILE [also upstream]; (3) ✅ a loopback source endpoint is never shipped in COPY_FROM_REMOTE to a non-loopback destination - `rpc_prepare_w2w_pull` forces the bridged copy (one-time WARN); loopback-to-loopback W2W (pure local fleets) is preserved; plus a 5s negative cache for unreachable peers in `copy_from_remote` (also bounds the SYN-timeout stall against a genuinely dead host to one dial per 5s). **Gates (2026-07-16, CPU loopback, Qwen3-0.6B):** (A) 2-worker all-loopback `-sm layer`: W2W still engages (100 COPY_FROM_REMOTE calls), temp-0 text byte-identical to the no-RPC reference; (B) mixed addressing (`--rpc 127.0.0.1:P1,10.5.5.1:P2`): guard WARN fires once, COPY_FROM_REMOTE drops to 0 (bridged), text byte-identical to (A); (C) protocol-level hammer (hand-built COPY_FROM_REMOTE): 400 distinct dead src endpoints = 400 real dials, worker fd count FLAT at 4 (was: +1 leaked fd per dial), then 200 pulls from one dead endpoint = exactly 1 dial + 1 log in 3 ms total (negative cache), worker alive; (D) worker under `ulimit -n 32` + 30 held connections: accept fails, logs "(attempt 1, retrying)", worker SURVIVES and serves normally after release (was: process exit - the exact .11 death). (4) ⬜ still pending: capacity-check the default memory-proportional split (clean per-device error when a share exceeds device memory) - the (a) fix.

BUG: autoweight fleet coordinator SIGSEGV during serving** — ✅ **FIXED same day (core-dump analysis): `gethostbyname` is MT-Unsafe and the fleet-status probes race it.** The systemd core (id 1347006, 6.7 GB, analyzed in gdb against the image's binaries via sysroot+solib-search-path) shows the crashing thread is an **httplib HTTP worker** in `server_routes::init_routes()::lambda#2 -> ggml_backend_rpc_endpoint_reachable -> rpc_probe_endpoint -> glibc NSS internals` — the `/fleet/status` reachability probe, NOT the decode path (the 2nd-request timing was coincidence: the user had the loading/fleet page open, each poll probes every RPC endpoint, and polls overlap across HTTP threads; glibc's `gethostbyname` returns a pointer into shared static state and is documented MT-Unsafe - concurrent calls corrupt its NSS machinery). The crash address sits right past `__nss_database_lookup` in libc, i.e. inside the resolver, and frame 1's return address is the instruction after the `gethostbyname` call. FIX: `rpc_resolve_ipv4()` via `getaddrinfo` (POSIX MT-safe) replaces both `gethostbyname` sites in transport.cpp (`socket_t::connect` + `rpc_probe_endpoint`); bonus hardening: the probe's `select()` now rejects fds >= FD_SETSIZE (FD_SET would write out of bounds under fd pressure) [gethostbyname-in-connect also exists UPSTREAM]. Gate: 60 rounds x 16 CONCURRENT `/fleet/status` polls (960 requests) against a loopback fleet with a killed worker in the probe set - all 200, zero faults, serving intact; loopback repro of the original decode-side theories had already come up clean (3 shapes), consistent with the real cause living on the HTTP side. Facts: run loaded fine (16 min, 7 RPC + 2 memory-capped CUDA stages; the #39 loopback-W2W guard fired at warmup as designed), request 1 completed clean (58 tok @ ~1.0 t/s, graphs reused 57); request 2 selected slot 3 by LCP similarity (sim 0.795, partial f_keep), logged prompt progress 0.95 at 15:31:17Z, then SIGSEGV ~16 s later (docker die exitCode=139 15:31:42Z; systemd core id 1347006, thread 22 in a libc memcpy-family frame, deeper frames unresolved). NOT the meta backend (layer split - meta not in the path). Loopback repro attempts NEGATIVE (trunc-V4 6L, np4 unified KV, LCP reuse f_keep 0.19-0.87 exercised): 3 all-loopback RPC stages; mixed loopback/LAN endpoints (guard-bridged handoffs live); A/B/C progressive-prefix sequences - all clean. NEXT: analyze the core - `sudo coredumpctl dump 1347006 --output=<path>` (+ chown), then gdb against /app binaries extracted from the 7ca338f6b image for the full backtrace. Coordinator restarted manually before that landed. Slice-aware worker-local sourcing: EP loads without streaming when shares move** — ✅ CLOSED 2026-07-17 (implemented + loopback A/B gate passed, see the tail of this entry; field EP exercise pending the next EP window). Originally ⬜ PARKED (filed 2026-07-16 from the user's observation that EP reloads re-stream despite full cache-rpc folders). Mechanics today: the worker tensor cache keys on the hash of the exact BYTES received - whole tensors in `-sm layer` (topology-robust) but SLICES in EP/tensor mode, so any `-ts` change moves every slice boundary and misses the whole cache; `--rpc-auto-weight` makes this chronic (shares depend on free RAM at load time -> nearly every load is a cold stream), and the 64 GiB LRU cap then evicts the stale slice generations. Even warm loads cost minutes (worker preads + re-hash-verifies its whole share - the #28 poisoned-cache integrity gate - plus per-tensor hash round trips and the <=10 MiB always-stream tier). FIX (the #26 scope note's deferred follow-up): workers with `--model-dir` hold the FULL GGUF locally - extend the local index so `get_local_tensor` can serve an arbitrary (tensor, offset, size) SLICE by pread from the GGUF (the index already stores file/offset/size per tensor; a slice is offset arithmetic for contiguous row ranges - check EP slice layout is contiguous in the source tensor, AXIS_1/AXIS_0 segments are row-contiguous per segment), with re-hash verify of the served slice as today. Payoff: EP loads become stream-free and share-change-proof on --model-dir boxes; the slice cache then only matters for boxes without a local GGUF. Also consider: quantizing auto-weight shares to coarse steps (e.g. 1/64ths) so repeated loads reuse slices even without --model-dir. **IMPLEMENTED (2026-07-17, proto 4.8): slice provenance end-to-end.** Client: the meta weight splitter announces, per member upload, which GGUF tensor + byte range the data aliases (`ggml_backend_rpc_source_hint(name, base)`, TLS, resolved via the reg-proc pattern, PARTIAL never hinted since those bytes are transformed); the RPC client derives per-chunk row geometry from its own set_tensor_2d gather (src_offset/row_size/n_rows/row_stride) and sends `RPC_CMD_SET_TENSOR_HASH2` = HASH offer + provenance (server_minor>=8 gated, mixed fleets degrade to the old offer). Worker: `--model-dir` index now also keyed by tensor NAME (persisted format bumped to `rpc-model-index v2`, v1 files re-hash once), `get_local_slice` assembles the exact slice by pread (strided rows supported) and serves ONLY on fnv-hash match - same-named tensors across files and stale files disambiguate/degrade safely. Surgical re-provision journal entries carry the provenance and replay via HASH2 (slice-sourced weights never touch the worker disk cache, the old replay would have abandoned to a full reload). Kill switch `GGML_RPC_NO_SRC_HINT=1`. Payoff: EP/tensor-mode `-ts`/auto-weight share changes no longer cold-stream on --model-dir boxes. Share quantization idea left on the table (mostly obsoleted). **GATE PASSED (loopback, Qwen3.5-9B 5.8 GB, `-sm tensor` over 2 --model-dir CPU workers, fresh slice boundaries both runs): hint ON `-ts 3,2` streamed 1.6 GB total (933+683 MB - only the sub-10MiB-threshold tensors and non-weight traffic); hint OFF control `-ts 2,3` streamed 5.6 GB (2283+3361 MB, the whole shares). -71% wire bytes on a boundary-moved load, temp-0 output coherent ('The capital of France is Paris.').**
- [x] **45. Worker-side cache housekeeping on load (bound cache-rpc growth)** — ✅ CLOSED 2026-07-17. Originally ⬜ PARKED (filed 2026-07-16, user request after a disk-full on the fleet; user: "the initial load should perform housekeeping on cache-rpc by the workers"). Problem: the `GGML_RPC_CACHE_LIMIT_MIB` LRU cap (default 64 GiB) enforces only on WRITES, so an idle worker keeps an oversized cache indefinitely, and every model/`-ts` change writes a fresh slice generation without reclaiming the superseded one (content-addressed by tensor-byte hash - a slice-boundary move = all-new hashes; see #44). Result: cache-rpc grows to the cap and stays there across models; on a tight worker disk this contributed to an OOM-adjacent squeeze. Facts that make it safe: the whole cache is rebuildable (verify-on-read gate; deleting any entry only forces a re-stream, never corrupts), and loaded tensors live in RAM buffers not open file handles, so pruning never affects a running model - only future loads. Design options (worker side, needs an image roll): (a) ENFORCE the LRU cap at startup / at each load start, not just on write - immediately trim to budget when the worker (re)connects; (b) a TTL/age prune (`--cache-max-age-days N` or startup `find -mtime +N -delete` equivalent) to drop stale generations; (c) mark-and-sweep tied to the load: the coordinator already names every hash the current model needs via SET_TENSOR_HASH offers - the worker could mark touched entries during a load and evict long-unmarked ones after, but must respect legit cross-model reuse (workers serve many models over a session, so don't evict a model you'll reload); (d) simplest + safe: a `--cache-clear-on-load` opt-in that wipes non-current entries at each fresh load (turn-key for single-model boxes). Recommended first cut: (a) enforce-cap-at-startup (smallest, always-correct, no new policy) + expose the current cache size in the beacon/GET_LOG so the fleet UI can show it. Compose already has WORKER_CACHE_LIMIT_MIB. Pairs with #44 (slice-aware --model-dir sourcing would make most cache-rpc bytes unnecessary in the first place). **IMPLEMENTED (2026-07-17, first cut = option a): `rpc_cache_enforce_limit` now also runs once at server startup (ggml_backend_rpc_start_server, before serving) so an idle worker trims stale generations before the next load; the discovery beacon carries `cache_mib=` (dir walk cached 30 s, `ggml_backend_rpc_start_announcer` gained a cache_dir param), `/fleet/status.discovered[]` parses it, and the fleet UI's Discovered table shows a Cache column. Worker image roll required to take effect. **GATE PASSED (loopback): 3x10MB dummy entries + GGML_RPC_CACHE_LIMIT_MIB=15 -> startup trimmed to the newest 10MB before serving; beacon carried `cache_mib=10` and /fleet/status.discovered[] exposed it.** **FIELD-PROVEN same day: the whole fleet rolled to rpc-worker-e3b4f019c and every production beacon now carries cache_mib (observed .25=16534 pre-hy3, growing to 29008 as its share streamed) - the disk-pressure view the task asked for. CLOSED.**

Fleet-only by design: single-box over-capacity (mmap paging / -ncmoe / ssd-streaming) is never gated; kill switch `LLAMA_FLEET_CAPACITY_CHECK=0`; resumes are never gated (capacity proven). Second run: .30 beaconed but was TCP-unreachable on all 3 advertised addresses (firewall) - skip-unavailable dropped it cleanly and the gate passed on the remaining 221 GiB. Exactly the designed UX.**

Pass cost grows as fast as tokens-per-pass. **VERDICT: MTP speculation CANNOT beat plain decode on a bandwidth-bound MoE pipeline - closed negative BY PHYSICS for this topology.** The no-pad acceptance fix stands (correct, and matters where MTP DOES pay: dense models on fleets, and VRAM-resident boxes where the 800 GB/s bus makes expert multiplication cheap - the single-box MTP prod configs). hy3 fleet ceiling = plain 1.9 t/s + the ~150ms/token overhead trim (~2.3 t/s max); further gains need quant (fewer bytes/token) or more fast RAM. Priority: behind #39 (stability > perf). Plain serving is the default meanwhile.

- [ ] **53. BACKLOG: env gate to strip reasoning/thinking tokens from chat HISTORY before templating** — filed 2026-07-17 (user request; refs a ChatGPT discussion link that could not be fetched - login-walled). Idea: reasoning models (GLM 5.2, hy3, DeepSeek, Qwen thinking) emit large <think> blocks; OpenAI-compat clients replay full assistant turns in messages[], so every request re-prefills and re-caches kilobytes of dead reasoning per prior turn. Server-side strip of reasoning from HISTORY turns (never the in-flight turn) is the DeepSeek-API-documented standard practice. Sketch: in llama-server's prompt assembly from messages[], for prior assistant messages drop (a) parsed reasoning_content fields and (b) inline <think>...</think> spans in content, behind `--strip-reasoning-history` / LLAMA_ARG_STRIP_REASONING_HISTORY (check first what this fork's chat templates already omit natively - the auto-parser separates reasoning_content, some templates never render it; the flag mainly covers clients that inline think-text in content). Payoff is OUTSIZED on the fleet: at 2-3 t/s prefill each stripped turn saves seconds of wall clock + KV cells + improves LCP slot-reuse similarity (stable prefixes). Caveat: first request after enabling changes the cached prefix = one-time prompt-cache miss; document. Effort: small-medium, server-only, no worker roll.
- [x] **54. CLOSED 2026-07-22: single-box V4 BEATS the fleet - 7.31 t/s vs 4.79. Drop the fleet for V4 on this box.** Live runs (2x V100, image b80ba3114, `run-ncmoe-54-measure.sh`): `-ngl 99 -ncmoe 99` warms 3.73 -> 6.64 t/s over 5 gens as NVMe reads fall 184 -> 4 MB/token; **`-ncmoe 37` (last 6 MoE layers' experts in VRAM) = 7.27-7.31 t/s steady, 1-2 MB/token disk** (+10% over all-CPU; prefill 13.6 vs fleet 12.5 t/s). Coherence gate passed on chat endpoint: Rayleigh answer word-identical to the fleet's, primes correct. ANSWER TO THE FILED QUESTION: the old 3.54 was measured with the fleet's local CPU worker holding 33 GiB - the "46 GiB RAM" constraint was self-inflicted. With the box's full RAM, page cache grows to 74 GiB and effectively ALL ~80 GiB of routed experts stay cached (routing skew + near-full residency), so the disk term vanishes: the fleet never had a disk-floor advantage, it had a RAM-starvation advantage over its own competitor. Theory autopsy: 50%-hit arithmetic never closed anyway (0.9 GB/token needs 340 ms at the measured 2.65 GB/s NVMe ceiling > the whole 282 ms token - old runs already sat at ~85%+ effective hit). Placement law learned: `-ncmoe N` experts follow their LAYER, and the non-CPU expert layers land almost entirely on GPU1 in a 2-way layer split - `-ncmoe 20` asked for 40 GiB on one card (OOM); budget = ONE card's free VRAM (~12 GiB = 6 layers), not both. PROD RECOMMENDATION (this box, V4): single-box `-ngl 99 -ncmoe 37`, mmap ON, `--no-warmup`, and do NOT run the local CPU RPC worker alongside (it eats the page cache that makes this fast). The fleet remains the answer only for models that exceed local RAM+VRAM (GLM-5.2). Caveat for the compose default: `-ncmoe 37` is tuned for 8k ctx np1 - more KV shrinks the 6-layer VRAM budget; back off toward 40 if OOM. — filed 2026-07-17 (user request). Working theory (from the day's analysis, UNVERIFIED by direct measurement): the box's 46 GiB RAM holds only ~half of V4's ~80 GiB of routed experts, so ~0.9 GB of the ~1.8 GB/token expert reads page from NVMe at ~2 GB/s = ~200-250 ms of the 282 ms token is DISK, not RAM bandwidth - the fleet wins by having zero disk in the token loop, not by adding compute. INVESTIGATE: (a) confirm the attribution directly - iostat/vmstat during a -ncmoe decode (expect ~1 GB/s sustained NVMe reads; if absent the theory is wrong and something else eats the time); (b) page-cache hit profiling (the ssd-streaming work has the tooling: GGML_SSD_STREAM_DEBUG hit-rate lines apply to the streamed path, -ncmoe uses mmap - use perf/cachestat or fincore sampling); (c) can placement close the gap without the fleet? ideas: put the HOTTEST experts VRAM-resident via -ot overrides (V100s have ~12 GiB free each after non-experts at -ngl 99; ~24 GiB of hot experts could lift the effective hit rate well past 50% given routing skew - ssd-streaming measured 73% hit at 30 GiB), or --ssd-streaming with GPU landing instead of mmap paging (its O_DIRECT + slot cache beat mmap-thrash before); (d) sanity: rerun the 3.54 baseline on the current image (it predates proto 4.6-4.8 work; number may have moved). SUCCESS = either single-box >= fleet (drop the fleet for V4) or a measured proof that the disk floor is unbeatable on 46 GiB RAM (then the fleet stays the V4 answer and the entry documents why). Pairs with #15 (ssd-streaming machinery) and the guide's placement decision map (diagram 1).
  GROUNDWORK 2026-07-22 (non-disruptive half done; live runs scripted, pending a fleet-stop window): (1) fleet re-baselined same-day: **4.79 t/s** decode, coherent 96-tok temp-0 (was 4.6). (2) NVMe floor measured (O_DIRECT random preads over the V4 gguf on nvme0n1): 256K QD1 0.67 GB/s | 1M QD1 1.67 | 1M x4 2.51 | 4M x4 **2.65 GB/s ceiling**. (3) The theory's arithmetic does NOT close: 50% hit rate (46 GiB/80 GiB by file fraction) would need 0.9 GB/token from disk = 340 ms at the 2.65 ceiling - MORE than the whole 282 ms token. The historical 3.54 t/s therefore already ran at a much higher EFFECTIVE hit rate (routing skew - consistent with ssd-streaming's 73% hit @ 30 GiB): at ~85% hit, disk ~100-160 ms + compute ~120-180 ms fits 282 ms. So the fleet does not win by "zero disk vs 200-250 ms disk"; it wins by a smaller margin against a mostly-cached single box. (4) KEY CONFOUND for the live rerun: the old 46 GiB figure was measured WITH the fleet's local CPU worker holding 33 GiB - stop it and ~70 GiB of the ~80 GiB experts fit in page cache, so single-box-with-all-local-RAM may beat 4.79. `run-ncmoe-54-measure.sh` (new) runs the whole live phase in one command - stops fleet, serves single-box V4 (NCMOE_LAYERS=99 baseline or =20 VRAM-heavy), measures t/s + NVMe GB/token per generation, prints output text (coherence gate), auto-restores the fleet on exit. Needs FLEET_STOP_OK=1 (takes prod down for the run; the 2026-07-22 session's harness declined to stop the fleet on its own).
- [x] **55. --ssd-streaming fine-tune vs the field** — ✅ DONE 2026-07-20 (commit pending in this session; CPU-tier measured, GPU-tier rides the next image). IMPLEMENTED: (1) parallel per-node demand reads on the CPU tier (defer + one ssd_read_batch, shared helper with the GPU path) - +17% decode (1.11 -> 1.30 t/s, V4-86GB, 12GB budget, rt=4; MB-scale random preads are latency-bound at QD1 while the NVMe does 2.3GB/s sequential); (2) LLAMA_SSD_STREAM_PREFETCH - background router-lookahead prefetcher keyed on the previous token's per-tensor routing, with filling-set+cv so demand never races a fill, plus prediction-accuracy and routing-skew counters. HONEST NEGATIVE: at practical budgets the previous-token prediction is REDUNDANT with the LRU (issued=0 - the cache holds ~5 tokens of history); at starvation budgets accuracy is 27.7% and churn is a net LOSS (0.87->0.63 t/s @2GB). Colibri's 71.6% comes from running layer L+1's ROUTER on layer L's hidden state - CURRENT-token prediction the LRU cannot pre-hold - which needs llama-graph surgery: that is the one remaining lever, kept opt-in as its harness. All variants byte-identical temp-0 output. FIELD (2026-07-20): Colibri (GLM-5.2 744B/40B-active, 370GB int4 experts streamed): 0.05-0.1 t/s @25GB RAM, ~1.8 t/s @128GB desktop, 1.07 t/s @RTX 5070 Ti, MTP 2.2-2.8 tok/fwd; mixtral-offloading (the LRU-expert-cache original, Mixtral-8x7B, GPU<->RAM not SSD): ~2-3 t/s on T4-class, spec-prefetch never left WIP; PowerInfer-2 (TurboSparse-Mixtral-47B on a PHONE from UFS): 11.68 t/s - but via activation-sparsity-retrained models (different contract). OUR GLM-5.2 RUN (the exact Colibri workload, sharded Q2_K_XL 227GB, CPU-only, 16GB budget, 46GB box): 0.33 t/s coherent = 3-6x Colibri's comparable 25GB tier; per-GB-of-cache 0.021 t/s/GB beats both their tiers (0.003-0.014). V4-per-GB (0.108) stays above GLM-per-GB - GLM's ~5.9GB/token expert demand is 4x V4's, physics not regression. Sharded-gguf streaming works. Their numbers are BELOW ours per-GB (0.3-0.4 t/s warm commodity vs our 1.66-3.1 t/s on V4-81GB @30GB cache) but their TECHNIQUES map exactly onto our parked follow-ups: (1) **router-lookahead prefetch** - they predict next-layer routing at 71.6% accuracy and readahead from a dedicated IO thread while the current layer computes; our #15 explicitly parked 'prefetch/overlap' - this is evidence the router signal is predictable enough to pay, and our decode-graph cache already gives a stable per-layer hook; (2) **learned hot-expert pinning** - usage-history file + auto-pin of hottest experts (our SLRU test was NEGATIVE but pinning-from-history is a different mechanism than scan-resistance: measure routing skew persistence across sessions first); (3) batch-union expert reads (we have this in the scheduler copy-optimization + prefill amortization - verify parity); (4) MTP spec composed WITH streaming (they get 2.2-2.8 tokens/forward at 39-59% acceptance - our #52 no-pad machinery applies). CONCRETE TEST VEHICLE once the download lands: GLM 5.2 Q2_K_XL (227 GB) on ONE V100 + 46 GB RAM + NVMe via --ssd-streaming - the exact Colibri workload on our stack, directly comparable, and independent of the fleet. Success = beat our own V4 streaming t/s-per-GB-of-cache on GLM 5.2 and demonstrably out-throughput Colibri's published numbers on comparable hardware. PHYSICAL FRAMING for expectations: V4 IQ2XXS 86.7GB has ~80GB routed experts vs 64GB pooled VRAM, so most experts stay in RAM regardless; the combo's real wins are (a) the resident-expert tail split across BOTH cards' free VRAM (layer split clusters it on one card - #54 one-card law), (b) genuinely parallel per-layer attention over NVLink (layer split is a sequential pipeline for a single stream - the 2nd card only adds capacity today), (c) KV headroom pooling. EXPECTATION MANAGEMENT: the CPU expert term dominates the V4 token (#54/#65: single-box -ncmoe 9.7-9.9 t/s BEATS the tuned 2-box EP fleet 8.99), so the ceiling is modest but the resident-fraction growth is real and compounds with context. APPROACH CANDIDATES (pick after repro): (1) sched-split path: let CPU-buffered expert trees split out of the meta graph and treat the meta<->CPU seam like an owner-broadcast boundary (LLAMA_META_EP_ONLY + ATTN_OWNER already model 'expert tier elsewhere'); (2) EP-local-member path: map -ncmoe-under-tensor onto the existing EP machinery as an in-process local CPU expert member (no RPC hop) holding layers 0..N-1's experts - reuses placement/boundary/defer code wholesale; (3) on success, the wizard's ncmoe mode gains a -sm tensor variant and its estimate budgets BOTH cards. REPRO VEHICLE (no fleet window needed for the abort capture): v4-trunc6-repro.gguf / hy3-trunc5 + LLAMA_TRUNC_ARR on devcuda with both V100s idle - record the exact failure signature first (meta assert vs foreign-view vs gallocr). GATES: no-ncmoe leg byte-identical (off-path untouched); combo coherence + resident-expert census; A/B vs `-sm layer -ncmoe` at EQUAL resident-expert count (isolates the TP-attention term), then at each mode's best tune.

Two fixes: (a) coordinator: distrust/cap shared-memory GPU devices in the auto-weight capacity model (an iGPU's usable single-allocation budget is far below its reported free mem; detect via device type/integrated flag in the beacon or probe a large test alloc at score time - .30 shows the capping machinery already works when reports are honest); (b) worker: an allocation failure must REJECT the request (error reply) instead of killing the process - a crash turns a sizing mistake into a fleet-wide retry loop. WORKAROUND meanwhile: run .11 with WORKER_DEVICES=CPU (or SYCL0,CPU) for EP - EP decode is RAM-bandwidth-bound and the iGPU shares the same LPDDR5X, so nothing is lost vs SYCL0 on this path (SYCL's win was layer-mode prefill, #59).

NOT memory pressure (measured 6.2/6.8 GiB free per owner at the abort). **2-minute CUDA reproducer** (replaces the 33-min fleet cycle, promote to gate 4b): hy3-trunc5-mtp + `--override-kv hy_v3.block_count=int:5` + 2 LOCAL members `--device CUDA0,CUDA1 -sm tensor -ts 1,1` + EP_ONLY/ATTN_OWNER=0,1/ALLOW_MULTI_LOCAL + a 4-layer 2-member artifact. Evidence chain: (a) placement OFF on the same config serves fine, placement ON crashes; (b) an IDENTITY-permutation artifact crashes too, so the defect is the core mechanism (remap/mask/AXIS_2), not the ranking; (c) the gate-4 audit CATCHES it once run on CUDA - `EXPERT_AUDIT: WARN member 0 layer 3 pair (tok 0, k 0): slot out of range (slot -67108864, member owns 96 experts)`, 48 violations in graph #2, and the garbage slots are `0xFC000000`/`0xFC00FC00` = f16 -inf = KQ-MASK BYTES sitting in the id-gather cell (the mask gather is intact; only the I32 ids gather is foreign); (d) trigger is the FIRST execution of a newly built graph shape - graph #1 always clean, the graph after a shape change carries violations, the next graph of that shape is clean again, and with a 1-token prompt the server SURVIVES while still logging 24 violations at graph #3 (so this also produces silently wrong tokens, not only crashes - re-examine the gate-3 "fp grouping" divergence in that light); (e) NOT the decode graph cache (`LLAMA_DECODE_GRAPH_CACHE=0`), NOT the delayed reduce (`GGML_META_NO_DELAY=1`), NOT lifetime recycling (corrupts even with the debug `ggml_set_output` pins); `GGML_META_MAX_GRAPHS=1` changes WHICH members are hit (both instead of member 0), i.e. it tracks the compute-container ring. **ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED 2026-07-26 (compute-sanitizer + per-node sync, on the 2-min reproducer):** ggml's CUDA `mul_mat_id` REQUIRES a token's expert ids to be DISTINCT, and placement violates that invariant. `mmid.cu:44` (`mm_ids_helper`): `int iex_used = -1; for (iex...) { nex_prev += expert_used < expert; if (expert_used == expert) iex_used = iex; }` - `iex_used` holds ONE lane per (token, expert) because top-k normally selects distinct experts, while `nex_prev` counts EVERY lane below the expert index. The placement remap maps every NON-OWNED lane onto member-local slot 0, so a single token routinely lands 8 lanes on slot 0; the recorded rows and the prefix-sum `expert_bounds` then disagree, the compact row space is inconsistent, and `quantize_mmq_q8_1` writes past its staging buffer. compute-sanitizer: `Invalid __global__ write of size 4 bytes at quantize_mmq_q8_1<(mmq_q8_1_ds_layout)1,(bool)1>`, address 233 GB outside the nearest allocation. Per-node sync (new `GGML_CUDA_SYNC_NODES=1`) names the node: `ffn_moe_gate-1 (MUL_MAT_ID)`, src0 `blk.1.ffn_gate_exps.weight` ne=[4096,1536,96], src2 `ffn_moe_ids_local-1` ne=[8,11]. The same host-side path has the assumption written down explicitly at ggml-cuda.cu:1997 (`break` after the first matching lane, then `GGML_ASSERT(ids_to_sorted_host.size() == ne_get_rows)`). NOTE the inversion: the foreign bytes the audit saw in the id-gather cell are a CONSEQUENCE of these OOB writes scribbling neighbouring buffers, not the cause - which is why every corruption-first hypothesis tested NEGATIVE: stale shadow ring registration (probe: shadow data vs current allocation = 0 mismatches), COMPUTE-flag disabling (probe = 0 hits), cross-stream races (new `GGML_CUDA_NO_CONCURRENT_STREAMS=1` = still crashes), CUDA graphs (`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_GRAPHS=1` = still crashes), star reduce (`GGML_META_NO_STAR=1`), delayed reduce, decode graph cache, ATTN_OWNER (crashes without it too - minimal repro is 2 local GPUs + EP_ONLY + placement), table upload (byte-verified readback, 0 mismatches), and OOB id VALUES (ids are in range at the point of use). CPU is immune because its mul_mat_id loops lanes independently, which is why gates 1-4 passed. **FIX (this is the v2 skip-sentinel item, now mandatory not optional):** teach mul_mat_id an id sentinel (e.g. -1) meaning 'skip this lane, write zero rows' and emit it for non-owned lanes instead of slot 0 - that removes the duplicates AND the dummy-expert traffic floor in one change (CUDA mmid/mmq + CPU + the host sorted path). NOTE a stopgap considered and REJECTED: giving non-owned lane k the slot (k % n_local) does NOT guarantee distinctness - an owned lane of the same token can already hold slot k, and which slots are owned is data-dependent, so no static table can avoid the collision. Reserving extra dummy slots per member would cost a full expert copy per lane (8 x 10.6 MB x 80 layers). The sentinel is the only correct route, and it is also the one that removes the traffic floor. Alternatively fix ggml-cuda to tolerate duplicate ids per token (upstream-worthy, more invasive). **FIX IMPLEMENTED + CPU GATES PASSED 2026-07-26:** skip sentinel (-1) shipped end to end - tables emit it for non-owned experts (llama-expert-placement), CUDA honours it in mm_ids_helper (excluded from nex_prev), both id maps are pre-initialised (inverse -> -1 so the scatter kernels skip, forward -> 0 so unused quantize rows read an in-bounds row nothing consumes), mmvq/mmvf return early, dst is zeroed so untouched rows cannot surface NaN, and the CPU binning loops (ggml-cpu.c + repack.cpp) skip it and zero dst. Getting there took three rounds, each located with GGML_CUDA_SYNC_NODES: ffn_moe_gate (helper index arithmetic), ffn_moe_down (forward map's uninitialised tail feeding the quantize grid), and the inverse map for the dedup path. **CPU loopback gates (deterministic vehicle, trunc-hy3, 2 loopback RPC members, temp 0, seed 1234, 24 tok, sha over the generated answer only - spinner frames and t/s banner excluded):** gate 1 HEAD-build vs sentinel-build with placement OFF = byte-identical (f61930b5) so the change is a NO-OP for every non-placement MoE model including MTP serving; gate 2 identity artifact = byte-identical; gate 3 hot-first under GGML_META_NO_DELAY=1 = byte-identical; gate 3b hot-first with the delayed reduce = ALSO byte-identical (the earlier recorded delayed-reduce divergence needed lopsided counts 32/160; uniform 96/96 does not trigger it). Placement verified ACTIVE in the placed gates via EXPERT_AUDIT (owned+skipped==computed every graph, 0 violations across 2376 pairs) - identical shas alone could not distinguish a pass from a silent artifact-load fallback. Harness gotchas worth keeping: this fork's CLI needs -st (not just -no-cnv) or it parks in console::readline_advanced burning a core; the loading spinner and t/s banner must be stripped before hashing; -DLLAMA_UI_GZIP=OFF is needed when /src is mounted read-only. **CUDA PPL GATE PASSED 2026-07-26 (settles the CUDA question):** trunc-hy3 on both V100s, wikitext 6 chunks, -c 512 - placement OFF 117822.3700 +/- 4127.49, IDENTITY 117822.1530 (+0.00018%), HOT-FIRST 117815.8321 (-0.0055%). Spread ~600x INSIDE the error bar, the same quality-neutral class the earlier PPL gate settled. With CPU byte-exact and CUDA PPL-neutral, the placed path is correct on both backends. **CONCLUSION on the CUDA audit violations: the gate-4 audit's end-of-graph readback is UNRELIABLE ON CUDA and its violation output there must not be trusted** - it contradicts GGML_CUDA_CHECK_IDS (no bad ids at the point of use), contradicts the CPU audit (0 violations over 2376 pairs), and contradicts the PPL result; the ggml_set_output pin evidently does not keep the flat gathers readable on the CUDA member shadows. Fix or drop that readback before relying on gate 4 again. STILL OPEN: on CUDA the audit reports id-cell violations on the first execution of a NEW graph shape (member 0, layer 3, mask-byte pattern), while GGML_CUDA_CHECK_IDS sees no bad ids at the point of use - so it is either a stale end-of-graph readback (the audit's own known weakness) or a CUDA-only defect. CPU being byte-exact means the DESIGN and the placement math are correct; what remains is CUDA-specific. Note GPU text comparison cannot arbitrate this (gotcha #4): the CPU baseline degenerates into repetition too, so the placed CUDA output looking degenerate proved nothing. **MTP REGRESSION CHECK PASSED 2026-07-26:** the skip-sentinel commit adds an unconditional `cudaMemsetAsync(dst)` to EVERY mul_mat_id call, including non-placement serving, so it was measured on the production spec-decode shape (Qwen3.6-27B-UD-Q4_K_XL-MTP, 2x V100, -sm tensor -ts 0.5,0.5, draft-mtp, -c 32768 for load speed, 200-token greedy generations, cache_prompt off). SAME BINARY both legs, toggled with the new GGML_CUDA_MMID_NO_DST_ZERO gate so build vintage cannot confound: memset ON tg 73.11/73.19/74.52 (mean 73.61), memset OFF tg 73.38/72.94/75.05 (mean 73.79) - a 0.24% difference against a ~2.8% spread WITHIN each leg, i.e. no detectable cost. Prompt-eval numbers are not usable here (89-137 t/s swing from prompt caching). NOTE these are not the production headline numbers (smaller -c, no mmproj, different prompt) - they are an A/B against themselves. **GATE 5 ATTEMPT 1 FAILED THE COHERENCE GATE 2026-07-27, root-caused and fixed:** the placed fleet leg loaded and served but generated pure degenerate output ('直径是' repeated for all 90 tokens) while the uniform leg on the same prompt produced correct physics in English. Cause: mmvq.cu:551 `channel_x = ncols_dst == 1 && ids ? ids[channel_dst] : ...` assigns into a UINT32, so the -1 skip sentinel wrapped to ~4.29e9 and the kernel read far outside the weights - big enough allocation that it returned garbage instead of faulting. That is the ncols_dst==1 SINGLE-TOKEN DECODE path. **Why every prior gate missed it: the CUDA PPL gate only exercises PREFILL.** CPU loopback was byte-exact (CPU has no such kernel), CUDA PPL was neutral to 0.005% (prefill only), the ownership audit was clean (ids are correct - the kernel misreads them), and the trunc-model decode degeneration WAS visible earlier the same day but was wrongly dismissed as truncated-model behaviour because the CPU control also degenerates. Fixed by returning early on a negative id before the unsigned conversion. Verified by DECODE comparison on trunc-hy3 over 2 CUDA members: placement ON and OFF now produce character-identical text, matching the CPU gate output. LESSON for the gate list: PPL cannot validate the decode path, and 'the model is truncated so the output is meaningless' is not a licence to skip reading it - compare against the control instead. **GATE 5 MEASURED 2026-07-27 - NO GAIN. Placement does not pay off on the record roster.** Identical footing both legs (same build, roster, -ts 21,21,46,50,27, prompt, n_predict=100, cache_prompt off, 6 runs each, workers restarted before each leg, 0 RPC errors in both): UNIFORM 3.42/3.41/3.68/3.48/3.29/3.27 = mean 3.43 t/s; PLACEMENT 3.36/2.95/3.40/3.16/3.47/3.39 = mean 3.29 t/s. That is -4%, and the ranges overlap (3.27-3.68 vs 2.95-3.47), so the honest statement is NO IMPROVEMENT, possibly a slight regression - against a projection of 8-10 t/s. Both legs passed the coherence gate (correct Rayleigh physics in English; the placed leg's answer was arguably better, citing the inverse fourth-power law). HYPOTHESIS for why: the fleet is LATENCY-bound, not expert-bandwidth-bound. 3.4 t/s = ~294 ms/token over 80 layers = ~3.7 ms per layer, which is the order of a GbE round trip plus reduce - consistent with the sum-of-stages law (#31/#49) and with the fleet's 2.0 t/s on 35B vs 108 t/s on the V100s alone. Placement optimises BYTES READ by the slow members; if the binding constraint is per-layer network/sync latency, cutting those bytes buys nothing. NEXT TEST before any further placement investment (v2 entropy budgets, GLM artifact): run one leg with GGML_META_TIMING=1 and split the ~294 ms/token into member compute vs reduce vs wire. If reduce/wire dominates, #75's ceiling is capped regardless of where experts live, and the roadmap should move to the latency axis (fewer members, bigger ubatch, spec decode over the fleet) rather than the placement axis. NOTE the feature itself is CORRECT - CPU byte-exact, CUDA decode character-identical to uniform - it simply does not help here. **Design findings from the same review (independent of the bug):** (i) the dummy-slot mechanism caps the projected win - every member computes EVERY (token,k) pair and non-owned lanes ride local slot 0, so a cold member still reads one full expert (11.3 MB) per layer on top of its owned ~0.24, turning the projected 8.5x member-RAM traffic cut into ~1.8x; a skip sentinel in `mul_mat_id` ids (write zero rows, read no weights) would make non-owned pairs actually free and retire the mask; (ii) per-member expert FLOPs rise ~5x - the baseline mul_mat_id dst is split so each member computes 1/n of the rows, while placement derives MIRRORED and every member does FULL-width GEMMs for all 8 pairs (harmless on the V100s, but the three CPU workers now do 5x the expert math; plan section 5 "cost is bounded by today's behavior" holds for pair COUNT, not width); (iii) the `-ts` vs artifact rounding is silent - `[25,24,54,58,31]` vs `-ts 21,21,46,50,27` moves ~0.5 GB of expert weight onto CUDA0, and the mismatch WARNING promised by plan section 5 is not implemented (`member_shares` is parsed, never compared); (iv) minor: `ffn_*_exps.scale`/`input_scale` have no load gate of the kind biases have, and the permuted load path bypasses the async/chunked upload (single-threaded - part of why a placed reload runs 33 min vs 22).

BUG CLUSTER (load-path robustness, all hit 2026-07-23 during #70 hy3 EP runs, build 1980084e6):** **(a)-UPDATE 2026-07-30: the poisoned-endpoint load now has its full causal chain captured live (see #71 crash-bug-(a) resolution): a mid-load manifest-stale endpoint failure SURVIVES to a health-200 serve and every decode -3s until the first request arms surgical->reload (which now self-heals on the fixed binary - validated on the real fleet). BUILD FOLLOW-UP: arm rpc_reload_pending at END-OF-LOAD when any endpoint is marked failed, so the serve reloads immediately instead of serving errors; also consider failing /health while an endpoint is poisoned.** (a) **manifest-stale wedge**: rpc_batch_flush_locked fails the ENTIRE endpoint on any batched-placement miss (ggml-rpc.cpp ~835-867, by design -> mark_failed -> reload/surgical re-provisions) - but without --rpc-reload nothing re-provisions and the load runs to 100% then wedges silently at context init (or warmup decode fails -3 on the marked endpoint). Fixes: stream missed entries as fallback instead of endpoint-fail, and/or make --rpc-reload mandatory in EP recipes, and/or loud abort when a load-time endpoint-fail has no recovery armed. (b) **stale manifests are STRUCTURAL when cache is at its LRU limit**: local worker (GGML_RPC_CACHE_LIMIT_MIB=65536, dir at 64G) evicted manifest-promised entries while writing a 68 GB slice -> guaranteed misses; cache limit should exceed the largest slice or manifest entries need eviction-pinning during a load. (c) **surgical re-provision fails on worker cache miss** (ggml_backend_rpc_buffer_reprovision 'worker cache miss for hash') then (d) **the in-process full-reload fallback SEGFAULTED** (exit 139, right after handle_sleep -> load_model on the dual-role config) - fresh container start with same config+cache loaded clean, so the crash is reload-path state, not config. (e) **65-second SET_TENSOR_HASH_BATCH stall** on the local worker (cache hashing+writing 68 GB blocks the serve thread) tripped the coordinator socket timeout -> healthy worker declared 'connection lost' (slow!=dead; the 8:44 first-load failure). (f) **MTP draft context over the meta backend intermittently fails llama_decode -3** ('spec draft: llama_decode[0] returned -3', preceded by 'backend offload failed for seq_id=0; using CPU sampler') and the failure cascade eventually drops the local worker connection mid-serve (worker alive, 0 restarts - buffers torn down, surgical re-provision needed). Hit on the hy3 dual-role EP + MTP=1 serve 2026-07-23; also the perf reason spec-over-fleet loses (see #71 stage 1). Repro ledger + timings in session log 2026-07-23. **(b) CONFIRMED AGAIN 2026-07-28 night (placement-under-deferral A/B): first PLACE=1 load after a day of uniform serving died at 31-32 min - 'batched placement: 67/311 entries missed on 127.0.0.1:50053 / 60/295 on .11 - manifest went stale, failing the endpoint' -> both endpoints failed -> 'expert placement: failed to allocate ownership tables'. Worker RESTART does not help (the cache index survives); the wedge is the 64G LRU limit being smaller than the slice + a second (permuted) layout's entries. MITIGATION THAT WORKS: recreate workers with WORKER_CACHE_LIMIT_MIB above slice+layouts (local 98304 with 105G disk free, .11 131072 with 630G free). TRAPS on the recreate path: local needs WORKER_PORT=50053 (compose defaults 50052); .11's compose image var must be given explicitly (WORKER_IMAGE=10.5.5.1:5000/llamacpp-cpu:rpc-worker-<tag>) or --force-recreate tries to pull bare 'llamacpp-cpu' and dies.**

- [ ] **60. HARDWARE: RDMA/fast-NIC upgrade for the older boxes (m.2 -> 10/25 GbE where applicable)** — filed 2026-07-18 (user plan). The stack is READY: the RPC transport ships an RDMA path (build flag GGML_RPC_RDMA, env GGML_RDMA_DEV/GGML_RDMA_GID, auto-negotiate at connect - 'TCP (RDMA auto-negotiate enabled)' in start_server) that has never had hardware to run on. WHAT THE MEASUREMENTS SAY THE PRIZE IS: (a) LATENCY, not bandwidth, is the fleet's wire tax - decode activations are ~15-30 KB/hop; EP was RTT-serialized (43 boundaries x RTT -> 2.56 t/s cap) and layer mode carries ~150 ms/token of hop/sync overhead. RoCE RDMA cuts LAN RTT ~300us -> 10-20us => EP potentially revives to worker-compute-bound (~5-6+ t/s V4-class single-stream), layer overhead trims too; (b) plain 10GbE-TCP only buys load-time streaming (~10x cold loads) - real but secondary. BUYING GUIDANCE: RoCE-capable NICs required for the RDMA path (used ConnectX-4 Lx 25GbE are cheap); consumer Aquantia m.2 10GbE does NOT do RDMA. Per box: .25 (i7-8550U) m.2 feasible; .30 (2012 i5-3210M) likely no m.2 - check ExpressCard/mPCIe or accept it stays 100-Mbit (it holds 4.9%); .15 laptop m.2 may be occupied by the NVMe; .11 + coordinator are where RoCE pays most (largest shares + EP members). SOFTWARE PREP WHEN HARDWARE LANDS: build worker+coordinator images with GGML_RPC_RDMA=ON, verify auto-negotiate, then re-run the EP A/B (the fused-boundary path + RDMA is the money measurement) and the layer-mode timing run. Pairs with #49 (whose RTT-serialized verdict this attacks head-on).

METHOD: steady decode load, per-worker [rpc-timing] GRAPH_RECOMPUTE exec avgs (via /fleet/worker/log where the flag is on), coordinator ep ewma/stats, subtract from wall token time -> residual per component. TARGET was ~-20%; **MEASURED same day (live serving, 497 ms/token): the hypothesis REVISED. .15 exec 115.3 ms (floor 87), local worker 100.6 (floor 78); summing measured+scaled stages ~= 461 ms -> coordinator+wire residual only ~36 ms (7%) - the imagined 150 ms of hops does not exist. Real split: ~72% memory floors, ~21% in-worker surplus (~+30% over floor per stage: node overhead/dispatch/send inside handlers - threadpool spin already measured negative in #28), ~7% wire. VERDICT: layer-mode is near its physics; realistic overhead trim <=10%. Remaining decode levers are quant, RAM, or #60 RDMA (which attacks EP, not this). CLOSED as measured.**
- [x] **62. Structured cache layout + manifest handshake: kill residual streaming on warm loads** — ✅ DONE 2026-07-20 (proto 4.10, loopback-gated; fleet gate rides the next roll). Implemented BOTH causes from the analysis: (1) weights of ANY size now cached - client streams unknown weights WITH their hash (new SET_TENSOR_HASH_DATA, fire-and-forget like SET_TENSOR, worker verifies the hash before the cache write) gated on buffer usage == WEIGHTS so activations never enter the cache; (2) manifest handshake - GET_MANIFEST returns every hash the worker serves locally (cache entries + --model-dir index, which now indexes ALL sizes) once per connection; manifest-known tensors queue client-side and land as ONE SET_TENSOR_HASH_BATCH (flushed from a single choke point in send_rpc_cmd_raw before any other command, preserving write order; batch miss = stale manifest = endpoint failure -> existing reload/surgical recovery). Old workers (<4.10) keep the per-offer path bit-for-bit. MEASURED (0.6B loopback, GGML_RPC_STATS): cold = 310 HASH_DATA streams (all cached incl. 512-byte tensors), warm = 1 GET_MANIFEST + 1 BATCH (310 placements, 96 KB) + ZERO weight bytes streamed + zero offers; temp-0 output byte-identical cold vs warm; 156 activation SET_TENSORs unchanged and uncached. NOT done (cosmetic remainder): the human-friendly cache-rpc/<model>/<tensor> folder view - hash-flat layout stays; revisit only if housekeeping ergonomics ever matter. Original filing: filed 2026-07-19 (user proposal: name cache entries cache-rpc/<model>/<layer>/<tensor> instead of hash-only, since layer-split tensor order is stable; user observes 'fully cached' workers still streaming on initial load). ANALYSIS - the residual bytes have two causes, and each needs a different fix: (1) **sub-10MiB tensors are NEVER cached** (HASH_THRESHOLD) - norms/biases/small projections, a few hundred MB per model, re-streamed EVERY load; this is most of what the user sees. Fix: lower the threshold sharply (an offer round trip ~0.3ms beats streaming 1MB ~10ms on GbE, ~80ms on 100Mbit - the old threshold predates the fleet) or batch small tensors into one cached blob per layer. (2) **per-tensor offer round trips** - even 100% cache hits cost one RTT per big tensor (~1800 offers for V4 = seconds, not bytes). Fix: the MANIFEST HANDSHAKE the user's naming enables: worker persists a per-model manifest (the #44 modelidx v2 format already has name+hash+offset per tensor!); at load start the coordinator fetches the manifest ONCE and diffs locally - zero offers for known tensors, one round trip total. DESIGN: keep the content hash as the integrity core (verify-on-read stays; dedup across models stays), add the structured PATH as organization: cache-rpc/<model-name>/<tensor-name> mapping to the hashed payloads (hardlinks or an index file - a folder per model also makes #45 housekeeping human-friendly: rm one folder = evict one model). Layer subfolders optional (tensor names already encode blk.N). Wire: one new command (GET_MANIFEST, proto 4.10) or reuse GET_LOG-style side channel. PRIZE: warm loads with genuinely 0 streamed weight bytes + offer time collapsed from ~1800 RTTs to 1.

- [x] **47. FIXED: `/fleet/status` memory poll poisons the COMPUTE socket -> spurious worker "connection lost" during serving** — ⬜ filed 2026-07-16. Distinct from the OOM class (#43): during the CUDA0+CUDA1+.11+.15 EP run, the coordinator logged `RPC worker '10.5.5.11:50052' failed in get_device_memory: connection lost or worker crashed` and every subsequent request 500'd - but .11 did NOT die from memory: `docker inspect` shows **ExitCode=0** (clean, not 137/SIGKILL), 29 GiB free, worker log totally clean (normal accept/close cycles, no assert/error/"giving up"), RestartCount=2, last restart 21:59:04 correlating with the decode failures. So .11's rpc-server is EXITING CLEANLY (code 0) mid-serve and docker restarts it, and the coordinator's `--rpc-reload`+surgical loop then churns. A clean exit(0) from rpc-server happens only via: (a) the SHUTDOWN command (`--allow-shutdown`, which the workers DO set) - is the coordinator's reload/surgical path sending SHUTDOWN to a worker it thinks is stuck? (b) the serve loop returning after the #43(2) accept-give-up cap - but no "giving up" line and accepts were succeeding. Suspect (a): investigate whether rpc_try_surgical / rpc_reload_and_resplit issues a SHUTDOWN or whether get_device_memory's failure marks .11 and the reload restarts it needlessly. NOTE this made the coordinator crash-loop on a fleet that had plenty of memory - so it masqueraded as the OOM story and I wrongly attributed it to memory twice. Repro: EP run, send several sequential requests, watch for a get_device_memory "connection lost" on a worker whose own ExitCode is 0. Evidence lives in .11 (ssh anyei@10.5.5.11, container llama-rpc-worker-cpu). Pairs with #46 (the UI showed green through this). **CONFIRMED (2026-07-16 code + live): the failure is the UI's own /fleet/status poll.** `ggml_backend_rpc_get_device_memory` uses `get_socket()` = the CACHED COMPUTE socket (ggml-rpc.cpp:2281) and on ANY failure calls `rpc_mark_failed(endpoint)` (2271), poisoning it. `/fleet/status` (the fleet/loading UI polling every ~2s) calls this on every RPC device; during active decode the compute path has in-flight ASYNC RPC traffic (proto 4.2 deferred PING/set_tensor responses) on that socket, so a synchronous GET_DEVICE_MEMORY recv reads the wrong bytes -> framing desync -> 'connection lost' -> mark_failed -> next decode fails -> surgical recovery. BOTH workers fail in the same 0.4ms because the poll iterates all devices in one handler pass (live: .11+.15 failed at 21:57:57.959576 and .959974). The .11 'exit 0 restarts' were the reload machinery churning a poison-marked-but-healthy worker, NOT real deaths. Correlates exactly with the UI being open. WORKAROUND: close the fleet UI during serving. FIX: the fleet-status memory query must use a throwaway/ephemeral connection (like GET_LOG/GET_SCORE/reachability already do via rpc_connect_ephemeral), NEVER the compute socket, and a poll failure must not poison the compute endpoint. This subsumes the 'healthy worker drop' framing above - it was never OOM and never a real crash for these cases. **FIXED (2026-07-16, 87a40c1e1): the fleet-status memory poll now uses a THROWAWAY connection.** New `ggml_backend_rpc_dev_memory_ephemeral(dev,timeout,free,total)` reg proc dials a fresh ephemeral socket (bounded reachability probe, then HELLO+GET_DEVICE_MEMORY with standard framing), never the cached compute socket or the failed-endpoint set; `fleet_dev_memory_cached` routes RPC devices through it. Gate (loopback 2-worker 0.6B): 15/15 decodes clean under a 10Hz /fleet/status poll flood while serving, zero poison events (pre-fix reliably poisoned them); status still reports real free memory (36790 MiB via the ephemeral path). Needs a coordinator image roll.

- [x] **37. BUG: V4 `-sm layer` over RPC shipped invalid-ptr graphs (fleet-killer); auto-weight caps ignored KV+compute** — ✅ **FIXED (2026-07-15, working tree; image build pending).** The original diagnosis ("the degraded no-pipeline retry ships invalid graphs") was WRONG — injection experiments on a loopback fleet proved the retry path is innocent; the corruption fires on ANY V4 `-sm layer` multi-worker decode, which is why that combo never worked. THREE independent bugs found and fixed, all reproduced on a 4-worker CPU loopback fleet with a truncated V4 (`LLAMA_TRUNC_ARR=1 --override-kv deepseek4.block_count=int:6`, ~60 s loads, Run-B-like `-ts 4,8,3,0` incl. a zero share):
  - **(1) RPC serializer shipped foreign view nodes** (the fleet-killer). The sched SKIPS view ops when splitting (ggml-backend.cpp pass 5) — they're no-op kernels — so a view whose data lives on another backend can sit positionally inside a foreign split's node range. Local backends never notice (host pointers are process-valid); the RPC serializer shipped them and the worker rejected the WHOLE graph (`[create_node] invalid data ptr`) then dropped the connection — the .25 "crash" in run B was this containment working as designed. DSV4 mass-produces the pattern: the memory-ctx input builder creates `get_kv()` state reshapes for ALL layers up front (they land in split #1's range) and hyper-connections view the PREVIOUS layer's `l_out` (land in the next split's range); dense/GLM/Qwen graphs don't do this, which is why only V4 died. Fix: `collect_local_nodes()` (ggml-rpc.cpp) drops view-op nodes whose buffer belongs to a different endpoint/device from the serialized subgraph — provably inert, since the sched rewired all same-split consumers to local input copies; an all-foreign (empty) subgraph skips the wire entirely. Coordinator-only fix — deployed workers keep working unchanged.
  - **(2) gallocr aborted on zero-byte tensors in empty chunks** (`GGML_ASSERT(tensor->buffer == NULL)`, first prompt, decode-graph cache ON): a small-ubatch V4 graph can place ONLY zero-byte tensors on a device (empty state-read slices + their input copies; measured `alloc_size=0`, chunk `max_size 0`) → zero-size chunk = dummy buffer with NULL base → first init leaves `data == NULL` (legal for 0 bytes) → the same copy tensor appears as a node of TWO splits → the second init tried to re-allocate it and hit the assert; the follow-on `ggml_backend_view_init` assert (`view_src->data != NULL`) is the same class. Fix: `ggml_gallocr_init_tensor` treats `buffer != NULL` as already-initialized (idempotent re-init), `ggml_backend_view_init` accepts NULL view_src data for zero-byte views, and the NULL-base log fires only when a >0-byte tensor lands in an empty chunk (real corruption).
  - **(3) auto-weight caps ignored KV + compute** (run A's clean OOM — the part of the original filing that was right): `apply_rpc_auto_weight` now reads the GGUF header for KV dims (MLA-aware via `attention.kv_lora_rank` + rope dims; per-layer `head_count_kv` arrays for hybrids; `-ctk/-ctv` type sizes) and scales the caps from weight space to weight+KV space, plus a flat per-device compute allowance = 2% of the model clamped to [512 MiB, 2 GiB] (env override `LLAMA_RPC_AUTO_WEIGHT_RESERVE_MB`), logged as `reserving N MiB KV total + M MiB compute headroom`. Verified: Qwen3-0.6B KV estimate EXACT (896 MiB @ 8k ctx = 28L x 8 kv-heads x 128 x 2 x f16), V4 MLA path sane (1024 dims/layer/token), too-tight fleets still bail safely to the default split.
  - **Hardening landed alongside:** `sched_reserve` clears `sched_need_reserve` only on SUCCESS (a thrown reserve used to leave the context claiming "reserved", so the next decode computed on never-allocated buffers); worker `create_node` rejection now logs tensor name/op/buffer handle; client logs when a worker returns NULL base for a known buffer. Debug enablers kept (env-gated): `GGML_RPC_DEBUG_FAIL_ALLOC=EP:SKIP:COUNT` (fail the COUNT allocs after SKIP on endpoint EP - reproduces compute-buffer alloc failures), `GGML_RPC_DEBUG_BUF=1` (buffer alloc/free lifecycle log).
  - **Gates passed (loopback, truncated V4 + 4 workers):** cache-ON load + serve clean, 3x temp-0 completions byte-identical to a single-CPU no-RPC reference; decode-cache-OFF also clean; Run-B injection scenario (PP compute alloc fails → non-PP retry → serve) end-to-end clean; Qwen3-0.6B 2-worker regression unchanged ("Paris. The capital of Italy is Rome"). Zero errors in coordinator + all 4 worker logs.
  - **REAL-FLEET VALIDATION PASSED (2026-07-15 evening, image `94b7ba5dc`, full 86.7 GB V4, 6 RPC devices + 2 V100s):** auto-weight logged `reserving 344 MiB KV total (n_ctx 4096) + 1654 MiB compute headroom per device`; split shifted as designed (V100s 32.7% -> 30.6% each, .15's 6 GB GPU 3.4% -> 1.7%, both memory-capped); load + warmup + first prompt + a 262-token generation all clean - ZERO invalid-ptr / connection-lost / alloc-failure lines (run A and run B failure modes both eliminated). Decode 1.5-1.7 t/s = the expected naive `-sm layer` baseline over 100-Mbit links (#31 sum-of-stages), not a regression - the EP topology (#28) remains the perf path.
  - **Residuals:** (i) `common_context_can_seq_rm`'s probe decode failure only downgrades, so the server logs "model loaded" even after a fleet-wide decode failure — acceptable for a probe, revisit if it masks faults; (ii) the old #30 "zero -ts share segfaults" again did NOT reproduce (zero-share worker loaded and served fine).

BUG: meta backend asserts at load when `-sm tensor` is combined with CPU-hosted weights (`-ncmoe` / partial `-ngl`)** — ✅ **FIXED (2026-07-15): supported, not rejected — same root cause class as task 37.** The sched skips view ops when splitting, so views of NON-meta tensors (CPU-hosted `-ncmoe` experts, partial `-ngl` CPU-layer outputs) sit positionally inside the meta split's node range; `ggml_backend_meta_graph_compute` materialized shadows for every node and asserted on the foreign buffer. Fix: pre-filter foreign-view nodes at the top of meta graph_compute (deterministic, so uid/content build caching stays valid) — the mirror of task 37's RPC `collect_local_nodes()` filter; the old narrow s_copy_main workaround is subsumed. Repro required BOTH partial `-ngl` AND `-ncmoe` (each alone worked). Gates (loopback, 2 RPC workers): exact repro shape (trunc-V4 `-sm tensor -ts 0.5,0.5 -ngl 4 -ncmoe 3 --no-mmap`) now runs and its temp-0 output is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the single-CPU no-RPC reference; regressions byte-identical: V4 `-ngl 99`, V4 `-ngl 99 -ncmoe 3`, Qwen3-0.6B dense tensor mode. Original filing:  Repro: single-box ncmoe profile (`docker-compose.ncmoe.yml`, working-tree edit, image `llamacpp-local-v100:latest` = `026ea0ae9`) with `-ngl 30 -sm tensor -ts 0.5,0.5 -ncmoe 13 -fit off --no-mmap` on V4-Flash: server dies during `common_init_from_params`'s warmup decode (the edit also dropped `--no-warmup`, so the first decode fires at load) with `ggml/src/ggml-backend-meta.cpp:1612: GGML_ASSERT(ggml_backend_buffer_is_meta(tensor->buffer))` inside `ggml_backend_sched_graph_compute_async`; the failing frame is `ggml_backend_meta_buffer_init_tensor_impl` (lines 773/1601 carry the same assert on sibling paths). Trigger analysis: with `-sm tensor` the meta device owns the split stack, but `-ncmoe 13` puts the first 13 layers' experts in a plain CPU buffer and `-ngl 30` leaves the upper layers on CPU too — the sched hands the meta backend a tensor whose buffer is a non-meta (CPU) buffer, and the init/split-state path asserts instead of falling back or erroring cleanly. Same failure class as the recorded task-10 residual ("unsupported-op CPU fallback in tensor mode aborts instead of erroring cleanly", meta.cpp:557). The committed profile (`-ngl 99 -ncmoe 20`, plain layer split, no `-sm tensor`) is unaffected — the 3.54 t/s single-box baseline stands. Decide: (a) support the combo — meta treats non-meta-buffer leaves as host-resident inputs, the way the ncmoe CPU experts already work under layer split; or (b) reject it cleanly at load with a real error message ("`-ncmoe` / partial `-ngl` not supported with `-sm tensor`") instead of a mid-warmup GGML_ASSERT. Until fixed: keep the ncmoe profile on `-ngl 99` layer split (or revert the compose edit).
