BUG: FA MMA kernel crashes on GTX 16xx (TU116/117 — cc 7.5 WITHOUT tensor cores)** — ✅ **DONE; CONFIRMED ON THE REAL 1660 Ti (2026-07-10 evening).** Worker at .15 pulled `10.5.5.1:5000/llamacpp-cuda:75-e08854d4b` from the #33 registry; the exact former crash config (35B, 5 layers on the 1660 Ti, **FA ON**, CPU coordinator) now completes coherently at **10.5 t/s — FASTER than the 7.8 t/s `-fa off` workaround** — and the worker process survives. Residual resolved (same evening): the worker log shows **NO fallback WARN** across both runs — including a stress run with warmup + 1.6k-token prefill (86.2 t/s pp / 10.2 tg, exit 0, worker alive) that exercises the largest MMA smem configs. So on the current image, **TU116 accepts the MMA kernel's shared-memory opt-in outright and runs the MMA FA path via its emulated HMMA** — the historical abort does not reproduce with today's smem tuning tables + CUDA 12.8 (the old worker image's build requested more than TU116's envelope, or the older driver/toolkit rejected it). The fallback machinery stays as armor: if any future config over-asks, the worker now degrades to the tile kernel instead of dying, which is exactly the #29(e) containment property for this error class. Upstream check came first per the plan: NO fix exists upstream (master still gates MMA purely on `turing_mma_available(cc)`; maintainer position in discussion #5329 is that tensor cores can't be queried via the CUDA API, only name/cc heuristics). Fix shipped here, probe-based (no name matching): (1) `fattn-mma-f16.cuh` — the `cudaFuncSetAttribute(MaxDynamicSharedMemorySize)` call is no longer `CUDA_CHECK`-fatal: on failure it logs a one-time WARN carrying the requested bytes + the device's `smpbo` (the diagnostic we lack for TU116), marks the device via new `ggml_cuda_fattn_mma_disable()`, and dispatches THIS call to the tile kernel (safe: tile has identical f16-K/V alloc geometry to MMA, so alloc/compute stay consistent); the duplicated softcap-branch setattr blocks were hoisted into one probe (per-kernel-symbol flag kept, `[2][GGML_CUDA_MAX_DEVICES]`). (2) `fattn.cu` — the kernel picker's turing AND volta MMA branches skip marked devices (`ggml_cuda_fattn_mma_disabled()`, decl in fattn-common.cuh); `GGML_CUDA_FA_NO_MMA=1` pre-marks all devices (worker-side knob — the TU116 worker keeps FA without fleet-wide `-fa off`); `GGML_CUDA_FA_MMA_FORCE_SMEM_FAIL=1` = fault injection to exercise the path on healthy hardware. (3) **Bonus latent-crash fix exposed by (2)**: `ggml_cuda_should_use_wmma_fattn` returned true for NVIDIA Volta but this tree compiles WMMA device code ONLY for HIP+rocwmma (`fattn-wmma-f16.cu:48` guard) → with MMA disabled the picker fell through to WMMA and died with `NO_DEVICE_CODE` ("no device code compatible with CUDA arch 700", async "unspecified launch failure"); the helper now returns false for NVIDIA-Volta with a comment. Also added the missing `cudaErrorInvalidValue` HIP shim (vendors/hip.h). **Verified (V100, Qwen3-0.6B, 300-token prefill so the MMA path engages, temp 0): baseline / NO_MMA / injected-failure all exit 0 and generate IDENTICAL text (~260 t/s each); injection fires exactly one WARN then disables per-device (was: process abort).** Remaining: confirm on the real 1660 Ti — build the `CUDA_DOCKER_ARCH=75` worker image (pull via the #33 registry), run 35B layers with FA on, expect the WARN + tile fallback and capture the logged smpbo/requested-bytes to finally size TU116's real envelope; then also close the #29(e) sub-case this crash exemplified.
- [x] **31. RESEARCH: smallest computable chunk for balanced heterogeneous distribution** — ✅ **DONE (2026-07-11): measured on the real fleet; laws + policy below.** **The split-policy curve** (35B-A3B, 2 hetero CPU workers i7-8550U/DDR4 + i5-3210M/DDR3, CPU coordinator, decode t/s): all-on-fastest **0.88** · speed-weighted 5:2 **1.33** · memory-default 2:1 **1.2** · **bandwidth-proportional 3:2 = 2.26 (optimum)** · even 1:1 **1.72**. **Laws established:** (1) *GPU-rich regime*: never distribute a model that fits fast VRAM (V100s alone 108 t/s vs 1.9 across all 5 machines — sum-of-stages + hops). (2) *CPU-fleet regime, single stream*: the pipeline **aggregates memory bandwidth** (decode is bandwidth-bound), so distribution HELPS and the optimal `-ts` is proportional to per-box **memory bandwidth** — not RAM size (the default; 2× worse) and not CPU specs. all-on-fastest is the WORST config in this regime — the naive "degenerate optimum" theory only holds when one box's bandwidth dominates the sum. (3) *MoE batching on CPU*: `-np 4` aggregate only +27% (1.83→2.33 t/s) — batched tokens route to distinct experts so expert reads don't amortize; batch-throughput scaling is NOT the CPU fleet's lever. (4) *Granularity*: layer granularity (~2% steps at 48 layers) is fine enough for pipelines once weighted right; finer sub-graph chunks cannot change the sum-of-stages law. **The qualitatively better unit is the expert (#28): composition law becomes MAX-over-contacted-workers instead of SUM-over-stages** — a slow box owning few/cold experts adds capacity while rarely gating a token (user's "smallest computable chunk" intuition, confirmed by this analysis; #28 will measure it). **Policy recommendations:** default memory-proportional stays (capacity-safe); CPU fleets should hand-set `-ts` ∝ measured per-box bandwidth (documented in guide §3b); follow-up idea: the #29(d) beacon gains a measured-bandwidth score so the coordinator can auto-weight (filed as part of #28/#30 work). Historical context below. **First real data point (2026-07-10 evening, fully discovered fleet — zero static config):** 35B-A3B `-sm layer` across i7-8550U/32GB (14.2 GB slice) + i5-3210M/16GB (7.0 GB slice), CPU coordinator: decode **1.2 t/s**, prefill 1.9 — the memory-proportional default hands a third of the model to a 2012 dual-core and the pipeline pays sum-of-stages for it. Also: first-load stream to these boxes ran ~15 MB/s (≈24 min for 21 GB; the .15 laptop did 130 MB/s — link speeds vary wildly across the fleet → #26 `--model-dir` matters even more). Baseline for the speed-weighted `-ts` experiment. **Data point 2 (same evening, ALL 5 machines / 6 devices):** 35B across 2×V100 (3.6+3.7 GB) + Ultra9 (7.5) + i7-8550U (4.0) + i5-3210M (2.0) + 1660 Ti (0.5), fully discovered, load ~85 s (caches warm): prefill 4.6, decode **1.9 t/s** — vs **108 t/s** for the same model on the two V100s alone. Quantifies the core single-stream law: pipeline time = SUM of stage times + hops, so adding slow boxes to a model that fits on fast hardware is a large net LOSS; memory-proportional splits amplify it. Distribution pays off for (a) capacity (models that don't fit anywhere), (b) multi-stream throughput, (c) #30/#28-style designs where slow boxes hold rarely-touched state instead of hot-path stages. Goal: find the work granularity at which a fleet of UNEQUAL machines stays balanced (slow boxes get chunks small enough to keep pace instead of bottlenecking the pipeline). Candidate granularities, coarse→fine, with what we already know: (i) **layer** (today's `-sm layer -ts`): 48 layers ≈ 2% granularity — likely already fine enough IF the split is speed-weighted; the observed imbalance (2-worker 35B ran at the slow box's pace) came from the memory-proportional DEFAULT ignoring speed, not from chunks being too big. Cheap first experiment: hand-tuned `-ts` ∝ measured per-box t/s, check the pipeline approaches the harmonic ideal. (ii) **attention/FFN half-layer**: doubles granularity, same activation-passing cost — needs graph-split support. (iii) **expert** (MoE, task #28): per-token dynamic, the natural fine unit — a slow box holding fewer/colder experts contributes without gating the token; granularity ≈ 1/(n_layers×k) of a token's work. (iv) **sub-matmul row slices** (tensor parallel): finest, but every layer needs a cross-machine reduce — task 17 measured even INTRA-box reduce overlap losing on Volta, and Ethernet AllReduce is ~100× worse; presumed dead end, document why rather than re-test. Also to investigate: dynamic rebalancing (work-stealing between workers vs static splits — per-token expert routing is effectively this for free), and whether a per-worker micro-benchmark at connect time (composes with #29 discovery beacon: workers report a speed score) can drive the split automatically. Deliverable: a doc section with the measured granularity/overhead curve on the real fleet + a recommended default policy.
- [x] **30. Worker-local GPU+CPU mix: KV cache in worker RAM, weights in worker VRAM** — ✅ **DONE (2026-07-11): "KV annex" implemented + gated on loopback.** Mechanism (no protocol change): the worker exposes its CPU alongside the GPU (`rpc-server -d CUDA0,CPU` — pre-existing flag); the coordinator (env `LLAMA_KV_WORKER_HOST=1`) detects worker-CPU RPC devices via a new side channel (worker prefixes `CPU|` in the GET_DEVICE_DESC reply — same fork pattern as `Meta[N]`; client strips it, exposes `ggml_backend_rpc_dev_worker_is_cpu` by proc-address so the device still reports type GPU and pure-CPU workers keep working), then (a) `llama_prepare_model_devices` reserves a worker-CPU device with a same-endpoint GPU sibling as a **KV annex** — excluded from layer placement; (b) `llama-kv-cache` allocates the KV of that worker's GPU layers on the annex buft (worker RAM); (c) `llama_context` creates the annex backend so the scheduler can run ops on annex-hosted tensors (found the hard way: "pre-allocated tensor in a buffer that cannot run the operation"). Attention for annexed layers runs on the worker CPU; GPU↔RAM handoffs stay worker-local. **Gate (loopback, 0.6B @ 32k ctx): weights 1137 MiB on worker-GPU, KV 3584 MiB on worker-RAM (annex line in logs), coherent output; decode 33.7 vs 36.1 t/s baseline (~7% cost) — 3.5 GB of VRAM freed for weights.** **Fleet-CONFIRMED (2026-07-11, image `44e70252a` on .15)**: `reserving RPC1 (i7-9750H CPU @ 10.5.5.15) as a KV annex` fired for the real worker; 35B `-ngl 10` put **5.2 GB weights on the 6 GB card (90% VRAM to weights)** with the attention KV (32 MiB — hybrid model, tiny KV; dense models are where the GBs move, per the loopback gate) in the laptop's RAM; coherent 8.5 t/s. **Edge found while baselining (filed, separate)**: `-ts 1,0` (a ZERO-share RPC device) segfaults after memory setup with annex OFF — pre-existing zero-share edge, not the annex code (inert with env unset); repro: `--rpc <worker-with-2-devices> -ngl 10 -ts 1,0`. Helpers: `llama_kv_worker_host_enabled`/`llama_rpc_kv_annex_for` (llama-model.cpp). Original spec below. Small-VRAM GPU workers (e.g. the 6 GB 1660 Ti) waste their capacity on KV+compute buffers: today a layer's weights AND its KV live on the same device, and the only alternatives are per-layer GPU/CPU splits (`rpc-server -d CUDA0,CPU`) or the coordinator-hosted `--no-kv-offload` (KV would cross the network every layer — useless remotely). Wanted: a worker-side placement policy (e.g. `rpc-server --kv-host`) that allocates the KV buffers of its GPU layers in pinned WORKER RAM while weights stay in VRAM — attention either reads KV over PCIe (decode reads are small) or routes attention ops to the worker CPU while matmuls stay on GPU. Payoff for the 1660 Ti box: VRAM holds ~2× the weight layers (the whole 5.7 GB for weights, KV in 64 GB RAM), turning a marginal worker into a real pipeline stage. Note: the single-box equivalent (`-nkvo`) already exists upstream, so the semantics are proven; the work is making the placement observable per-device through the RPC boundary.

Distributed: worker fleet management (down node → redistribute)** — ✅ **CLOSED (2026-07-15): (a)(b)(c)(d)(e) all done.** Final (c) gap found+fixed while re-verifying on today's tree: `rpc_reload_and_resplit` only filtered unreachable devices when `--device` was EXPLICIT — with plain `--rpc`/discovery (i.e. the real fleet) `params.devices` is empty, llama re-discovered the dead endpoint at reload and every attempt failed on its buffers (the 07-13 matrix must have run with --device; the fleet soak passed via surgical, masking it). Fix: when devices is empty, synthesize llama's default list (RPC GPUs first, device_id-deduped locals, iGPU only without dGPUs, trailing nullptr) so the reachability probe filters it — positional -ts stays aligned by construction; `rpc_try_surgical` similarly probes all registered RPC devices when params.devices is empty (it never armed for --rpc-only configs). Surgical on layer-split topologies falls back to the full reload by design (meta member buffers only) — now logged explicitly. Re-verified kill matrix (loopback 0.6B, 2 workers, --rpc-reload): permanent death -> 500 + "dropping unreachable device RPC1" + reload onto survivor, same PID, byte-identical; killed-then-returned worker -> surgical attempt, clean fallback, recovery, same PID, byte-identical. Real-LAN multicast discovery: confirmed implicitly by the 2026-07-15 fleet runs (5 workers discovered through the physical switch). Surgical default-ON since the fleet soak (opt-out LLAMA_RPC_NO_SURGICAL). History below:  — 🔄 in progress; **(a) DONE (2026-07-10)**. Today there is NO *runtime* fault handling: a dead worker aborts the coordinator mid-session (guide §7). Wanted, in increasing difficulty: (a) ✅ (b) ✅ (see below) (c) ⬜ — (a) ✅ **load-time degradation — DONE (2026-07-10)**: new `--rpc-skip-unavailable` flag (env `LLAMA_ARG_RPC_SKIP_UNAVAILABLE`; `common/arg.cpp` + `common/common.h`) — unreachable `--rpc` servers are dropped with a WARN (per-server + summary + a `-ts`-is-positional caveat) and the default memory-proportional split recomputes over the survivors + local devices; all-workers-down degrades to local-only (loudly); "model no longer fits" surfaces through the normal allocation error path. Default behavior unchanged (fail-fast, now with a hint naming the flag). Order-independent by construction: the flag is pre-scanned (argv + env) in `common_params_parse_ex` before any handler runs, because the `--rpc` handler registers devices the moment it executes (possibly during env-var handling); registration stays parse-time-immediate on purpose — deferring it would break `--device RPC[...]`/`-ot ...=RPC[...]` name resolution. Verified (CPU loopback, live worker :50900 + dead :50999, 0.6B): 7-case matrix all pass — fail-fast unchanged, flag before/after `--rpc`, env×env, all-dead→local-only, happy path; survivor genuinely used (all layers assigned `RPC0`); warnings visible on `llama-server` at default verbosity (the new CLI hides parse-time WRN by design — `cli.cpp:370` starts at LOG_LEVEL_ERROR; ggml's own `E Failed to connect` still shows there, and `-v` placed *before* `--rpc` reveals the full warnings). Docs: guide §6/§7, `env-gates.md` §7. Remaining: (b) ✅ **coordinator drop-survival — DONE (2026-07-11)**: all 18 client-side `RPC_STATUS_ASSERT` abort sites converted to endpoint-poisoning containment (`rpc_mark_failed`, one actionable log line; ops on a dead worker degrade to no-ops with zeroed outputs; `graph_compute` fast-fails poisoned endpoints so decodes return clean errors); fixed the SIGPIPE process-kill hiding behind the old aborts (transport sends now `MSG_NOSIGNAL`); llama-server on a lost worker sends proper error responses to in-flight requests (verified: HTTP client received `{"error":{"code":500,"message":"Compute error."}}`) then exits code 42 after a 2 s flush window (new `ggml_backend_rpc_any_endpoint_failed` proc-address API) — with `restart: always` + `--rpc-discover` the coordinator reloads and re-splits across live workers automatically = self-healing fleet (a working sub-case of (c)). Verified on loopback: worker killed mid-generation → clean per-request error, no abort/core/SIGPIPE; restart+rediscovery cycle recovers; server exit 42 with flushed 500. Deliberate scope note: a connection lost between a successful compute and the logits read can produce one zero-logits token before the failure surfaces (void backend APIs) — closed by (c)-era work. In-place reconnect without reload is NOT possible under per-connection buffer ownership (the worker frees everything with the connection) — that redesign is (c); (c) **runtime redistribution** — on permanent worker death, re-load the lost layer shares onto surviving devices and resume (KV for lost layers is gone → affected slots restart their generations; needs the scheduler to support re-splitting a live model). **DONE (2026-07-13) as in-process reload-and-resplit: new `--rpc-reload` server flag (env `LLAMA_ARG_RPC_RELOAD`).** On worker loss the server fails in-flight requests (clean 500s), unloads the model via the sleeping-state machinery, clears the RPC failure marks (new reg procs `ggml_backend_rpc_reset_failed_endpoints` / `ggml_backend_rpc_dev_reachable`), probes every configured device and reloads across the reachable ones - dead workers drop out WITH their positional -ts shares; the pre-failure device list is kept as the probe base so a worker that returns is re-included by the next failure-triggered reload; if no worker is reachable it loads local-only (loudly, matching --rpc-skip-unavailable semantics), and if even that fails (fleet-sized models) it retries every 10s until a load succeeds. Same PID, HTTP state and queue throughout - no restart policy needed (default behavior unchanged: exit 42 for the restart loop). Closed the #29b known gap on the way: a worker lost between a successful compute and the logits read yielded ONE silent zero-logits token (ret==0) - decode now promotes any_endpoint_failed to a compute error before sampling. ALSO found+fixed a pre-existing SEGFAULT the kill-tests exposed: ggml_backend_sched ignored ggml_gallocr_reserve_n's return, so a worker death BEFORE a sched re-reserve (e.g. first decode) dereferenced NULL buffers in gallocr_alloc_graph and killed the coordinator before any 29b containment could react (prior kill-tests always hit mid-decode with buffers already reserved); reserve failure now fails the decode cleanly. Kill-test matrix (0.6B, 2 loopback workers, CPU build): worker dies mid-serving -> 500 + in-process reload onto survivor + next request OK (same PID); dead worker RETURNS -> re-included on the next reload; BOTH workers dead -> loud local-only degrade, serving continues; no flag -> unchanged 500 + exit 42. Remaining refinement (filed, not blocking): surgical share re-split without a full reload (keep unaffected KV; only re-upload the lost share) - worthwhile once the dedicated-attention topology makes worker shares stateless expert weights. **BUILT (2026-07-14, bc5137276) - EXPERIMENTAL, opt-in LLAMA_RPC_SURGICAL=1.** Mechanism: a per-buffer client-side journal records weight-class writes (big: offset/size/HASH - replayed via SET_TENSOR_HASH against the restarted worker's local cache; small strided per-row writes: literal bytes spilled to an anonymous temp file, GGML_RPC_JOURNAL_MAX_MIB cap), plus buffer clears and quantized-pad inits; on the worker's return at the same endpoint the buffers are re-created, the journal replays (read-back hash-verified under GGML_RPC_REPROVISION_VERIFY=1), and the meta backend rebases its shadow registrations onto the new bases (buffer usage now propagates meta->members so compute members are exempt; meta member-alloc failure downgraded from abort to clean error - it fired as a coordinator SEGFAULT-class abort when a sched re-reserve hit a dead endpoint). Server side: --rpc-reload tries surgical first (waits LLAMA_RPC_SURGICAL_WAIT_S=120 for the endpoint to return), falls back to the full reload on ANY failure. MEASURED: recovery ~2s after the worker returns (7-8s including a 5s simulated restart) vs minutes for the reload; server, HTTP state and other members untouched. WHY STILL OPT-IN: on 6L-truncated V4 the post-surgical generation is self-consistent but deterministically DIFFERENT from the fresh state (wide-margin token flip, similar vocabulary) even though journaled regions verify byte-exact, clears are replayed and MIRRORED non-weight statics are sibling-restored; a no-kill control reproduces the fresh output, so the delta is real. Remaining suspects for next session: slot/prompt-state interaction with the failed in-flight request, non-mirrored graph-computed statics (deepseek4 hash-layer tables' classification), a rebase gap. PPL-equivalent VERDICT (2026-07-14, teacher-forced top-30 distributions over 22 positions via the server, identical prompts pre-kill vs post-surgical): top-1 agreement 11/22, mean |dlogprob| on shared tokens 1.81 nats (max 7.7) - a LARGE distributional shift, NOT a benign rounding-path change (those sit ~1e-3). The surgical restore corrupts or misses real state; stays opt-in. Next diagnostic: whole-buffer differential of the restored worker pre-kill vs post-surgical (GET both full buffers and diff regions against the journal map), plus classify deepseek4's graph-computed statics (hash-layer tables) - the harness lives in the session notes (workers sw1/sw2 :50181/:50182, 6L V4, port 18095). **RESOLVED (2026-07-14): the delta was a journal KEY COLLISION - fixed, gate now byte-exact.** `rpc_buffer_journal::sets` was keyed by the TENSOR-relative write offset; every tensor's first chunk is offset 0 (and the 32 MiB chunked uploads reuse the same offset series), so on a member buffer holding many shadow tensors the std::map last-write-wins dedup silently dropped all but the last tensor's entries per key - the replay restored only a sliver and left the rest of the restarted worker's share as fresh zero pages (attention is CUDA0-dedicated = intact; RPC1's expert share mostly zeroed = exactly the observed self-consistent-but-shifted output). The read-back verify passed because it only checks SURVIVING entries - it could never see a dropped one. Fix: key by buffer-relative start (root offset-from-base + write offset); a rewrite of the same region still dedups. Added tripwires: the restore log now prints journal COVERAGE (a weight buffer far below 100% = lost or journal-bypassing writes), a sibling-restore count, and a post-rebase stale-shadow scan. Verified (6L V4 harness): weights member buffer 5904 MiB restored as 163 cache-replayed + 122 literal writes, journal covers 100.0%, verify 285 regions 0 mismatches, sibling-restores 0 (dsv4/KV statics are CUDA0-dedicated, so nothing mirrored lives on worker non-weight buffers - as designed), 0 stale shadows; post-surgical output BYTE-IDENTICAL to the fresh baseline ("raeli kinain...") across 3 kill/restart cycles on 2 server instances (recovery ~14 s with the full read-back verify armed). Stays opt-in pending a fleet soak, but the known corruption is closed. FLEET SOAK KILL-TEST PASSED (2026-07-15, full 86.7GB V4, real .11 worker docker-restarted mid-serving): clean 500, surgical re-provision of the 48GB share from .11's local cache, TOTAL recovery 121s (vs ~10+min reload; cost = one worker's cache re-verify), post-recovery output byte-identical to baseline. Workers needed no changes - coordinator-side env only. (a) is cheap and immediately useful for fleets like ours where laptops come and go; (c) is a real project. Pairs with #26 (worker-local sourcing makes redistribution loads fast). **Plus (d) worker presence broadcasting — ✅ DONE (2026-07-10)**: `rpc-server -a/--announce` (+ `--announce-group ADDR:PORT`) multicasts a versioned text beacon every ~2 s (`llama.cpp-rpc/1 port=… proto=… devs=… free_mib=…`; coordinator derives the IP from the datagram source, TTL 1, egress pinned to the `-H` interface so exposure never widens); coordinator `--rpc-discover` (+ `--rpc-discover-group`, envs `LLAMA_ARG_RPC_DISCOVER[_GROUP]`) listens 2.5 s post-parse, registers every unique worker heard, warns-and-continues when none found, and always uses skip-unavailable semantics (a worker can vanish between beacon and connect). Plumbing: beacon+listener in `ggml-rpc/transport.{h,cpp}` (default group `239.255.77.99:50153`, listener joins on INADDR_ANY + loopback so same-host workers bound to 127.0.0.1 are heard), C API `ggml_backend_rpc_start_announcer`/`ggml_backend_rpc_discover` exposed via registry proc-addresses (DL-build-safe); `add_rpc_devices` gained a process-wide endpoint dedup (same worker in `--rpc` AND discovered → registered once; failed registrations un-tracked for retry). Verified (loopback CPU build, 0.6B): discovery→load→generate on the discovered worker; `--rpc` + `--rpc-discover` dedup (single RPC0); no-workers → clean WARN + local run; custom group both sides works; mismatched group finds nothing (correct). Real-LAN multicast (through the physical switch) still to confirm on the fleet. Docs: guide §1/§2, env-gates §7. Free-mem/devs already in the payload — the #31 speed-score extension has a natural home. Original design note: default stays exactly today's behavior (static `--rpc` list, no broadcast); trusted networks only. **Plus (e) worker error containment — ✅ DONE (2026-07-10 evening; the observed 1660 Ti crash itself turned out to be #32, fixed separately).** Design: CUDA errors are funneled through `ggml_cuda_error` (every `CUDA_CHECK`/`CUBLAS_CHECK`), which now **throws** `std::runtime_error` instead of aborting when `GGML_CUDA_ERROR_CONTAIN=1` — armed automatically by `rpc-server` at startup (explicit `=0` restores abort; coordinators/tools never arm it, zero default-behavior change; the unwind crosses C++ frames only). `rpc_server::graph_compute`/`graph_recompute` route through new `compute_contained()`: catches the exception (or a non-SUCCESS status — both sites previously `GGML_ASSERT`ed even on a *returned* failure), logs, **drops only that connection** (stored graph nulled so a failed graph is never RECOMPUTEd), and a consecutive-failure counter (reset on success) treats 3 strikes as a poisoned backend → `std::_Exit(1)` (no atexit/CUDA teardown that could hang) so `restart: always` resurrects a clean process. Coordinator side: `ggml_backend_rpc_graph_compute` send failures now return `GGML_STATUS_FAILED` (+ uid invalidation, never RECOMPUTE across a reconnect) instead of `RPC_STATUS_ASSERT`-aborting. Fault injection: `GGML_CUDA_INJECT_COMPUTE_FAIL` (`N>0` = fail Nth-and-after; `N<0` = fail only |N|th). **Verified (V100 loopback, injected CUDA errors): (i) isolated error — worker logs `failure 1/3`, drops the connection, STAYS UP, and a fresh coordinator session on the same process loads + generates normally (exit 0); (ii) poisoned backend — failures 1/3, 2/3, then clean self-exit on the 3rd; (iii) plain local-GPU run unaffected.** Known remaining gap (deliberate, = scope of (b)): the *coordinator* still aborts when the dropped connection surfaces at the next blocking RPC (set_tensor_hash/synchronize recv — void APIs, ~40 `RPC_STATUS_ASSERT` sites); restarting it is cheap against the worker's warm cache. Guide §6 + env-gates §7 updated.

On the way, found+fixed TWO fleet-wide correctness/stability bugs that masqueraded as split bugs for a whole day: (1) the rpc-server tensor cache (`-c`) served UNVERIFIED entries — killed loads left truncated files that poisoned every later load (upstream has the same hole); now atomic tmp+rename writes + re-hash verify-on-read with self-healing fallback (`05a0634c3`); (2) cache-miss loads build model-sized dirty page cache on top of the weights and can OOM a tightly-sized worker; now fsync+fadvise per entry + clean load failure instead of coordinator abort (`c18e3dbbc`). Debug enablers: `LLAMA_TRUNC_ARR=1` + `--override-kv <arch>.block_count` (minutes-scale truncated reproducers), `GGML_META_NO_DELAY=1`, `GGML_RPC_STATS=1`. V4 split math verified clean (loopback dup-device token-identical to single-CPU). **Perf lever 1 landed (2026-07-12, RPC proto 4.4): batched W2W reduce pulls.** The allreduce fallback's pulls of one butterfly step now overlap on the wire: all fences captured before any COPY_FROM_REMOTE is sent (early fences are safe - they already cover the producing compute - and keeping sibling pulls out of each fence is what removes the serialization), then all requests, then the acks. Required a server fix: 4.3 held the per-process exec lock across the whole pull, so two crossing in-flight pulls ABBA-deadlocked ACROSS worker processes; 4.4 fetches without the lock and takes it only for the local write (same shape as GET_TENSOR_FENCED). Client batches only when both ends are >= 4.4 - mixed fleets keep the sequential path (validated vs a real 4.3 server: no deadlock/crash; old-dst-pulls-new-src degrades to the bridged copy until the roll). Gates: 0.6B loopback 18.4 t/s coherent; truncated-V4 meta token-identical to single-CPU. **Fleet A/B done (2026-07-13, fleet rolled to 4.4): V4 2-worker .11+.15 `-ts 3,2` reduce 40 -> 25.5-28.5 ms/graph piece (-35%); decode unchanged (0.98 timed / 1.20 untimed) because compute (155-223 ms/piece, ~85% of token time) dominates -> multi-build subgraph cache is now decisively the next lever.** **Perf lever 2 landed (2026-07-13, c20e432b4): multi-build subgraph cache - builds keyed by outer-graph content hash (sched pieces carry a fresh uid every token so uid equality never matched; every call redid the full client build, ~30ms/piece), shadow-ring rotation as validity horizon, init_tensor memo against arena growth. Fleet 2-worker V4 0.98 -> 1.16 timed / 1.20 -> 1.25 untimed, 128/128 hits; token-identical to previous commit on 0.6B + V4-trunc loopback. Remaining ~800ms/token is per-boundary worker compute (mirrored attention runs fully on every member) -> next levers: increment 3 placement, reduce/compute overlap.** **Increment 3 prototype landed (2026-07-13, fd5bccf38): LLAMA_META_ATTN_OWNER=<j> dedicates DSA attention weights+cache to one member via DEGENERATE splits (zero-size slices disable non-owner compute; dot-dim exit derives PARTIAL so the existing reduce broadcasts x+0=x); derivation gains degenerate-state escapes; zero-slice disable now FILLs 0 (SCALE*0 turned recycled NaN/Inf into poison). Validated: token-identical to mirrored on CPU loopback under NO_DELAY; Meta(CUDA0,RPC,RPC) coherent through 30-layer truncations. The depth corruption was FOUND+FIXED (7c1824615): the delayed-AllReduce matcher stepped past consumers without checking use count, so hyper-connection streams read UNREDUCED attn_out (zeros on non-owners) and members drifted per layer. **FULL V4 COHERENT on Meta(CUDA0=V100 attention owner, .11+.15 experts `-ts 0,3,2`): correctness milestone of increment 3 DONE.** Boundary optimizations (d23d9795a: single-computer reduces become broadcasts - no zeroing/butterfly; island-internal deg-x-deg matmuls stay owner-local, only attn_output_b exits broadcast) took boundaries 26.8 -> 21.5/graph and reduce 82-88 -> 40 ms/graph. **DECODE 1.80 t/s untimed - the EP design now beats the mirrored fleet (1.25) by 44%; single-box bar 3.54 is the next target. Computers-only moe reduce RESOLVED NEGATIVE (4e6e6c1b5: 1.34 vs 1.80 t/s - star hops through the local zeroed CUDA member beat W2W fenced pulls; full butterfly kept, note in code). Slice-aware sizing RESOLVED BY MEASUREMENT: the 67GB reserve was the DEFAULT ub=512 DSA worst case - at -ub 256 the reserve is 258 MiB and -c 4096 runs fine (prefill 7.55 t/s on a 203-token prompt, decode 1.82 t/s - best yet). Recommended config: -c 4096 -ub 256 -b 256. Uniform per-member allocation remains as virtual-memory hygiene debt only. .25 cold-share experiment RESOLVED NEGATIVE (2026-07-13): -ts 0,3,2,0.5 (9% experts on the 100-Mbit box) = 0.42 t/s vs 1.82 without it, coherent - the cost is ~16ms PER BOUNDARY (link latency x ~112 boundaries/token), not per byte of experts, so NO share is small enough: slow-LINK boxes must stay OUT of the synchronous decode ring entirely. Their capacity role needs an async mechanism (network-backed expert paging, batch-only participation) - increment-4 territory. Note V4 does not need .25 for capacity in the dedicated config (experts 80GB fit .11+.15 at 3:2). Remaining levers: dispatch pipelining, EP batching (inc 4).** **Perf lever 3 landed (2026-07-13): STAR REDUCE - the dispatch-pipelining lever.** With a local member (the CUDA attention owner) available as root, each multi-computer reduce boundary now does ONE batched read of every computing member's partial (new RPC reg proc `ggml_backend_get_tensor_batch`: all GET_TENSOR requests go out before any response is read, so the workers' compute tails and their transfers overlap; in-order serving makes each read an implicit fence on the producing compute), sums the partials on the host, and broadcasts identical result bytes with fire-and-forget SETs. The fold+butterfly's 2-3 sequential blocking round trips, CUDA ADD launches and worker-side ADD turnarounds per boundary are gone, and zero-share members stop paying FILL kernels. Client-only: no protocol bump, deployed worker images unaffected; all-wire fleets (no local root) keep the butterfly; `GGML_META_NO_STAR=1` restores it. Also fixed: batch procs were looked up only from member 0's reg, so Meta(CUDA0,RPC,...) never found cpy_batch. Gates: 12L-truncated V4 loopback Meta(CUDA0,RPC,RPC) token-IDENTICAL star vs butterfly (host f32 sum keeps the fold order; fp add is commutative), and the FULL 86.7GB V4 fleet outputs are byte-identical star vs butterfly. Fleet perf (CUDA0 owner + .11/.15 `-ts 0,3,2 -c 4096 -ub 256 -b 256`): decode **2.08 / 2.24 t/s** (two runs) vs same-binary butterfly controls 1.30/1.69 and the prior best 1.82; prefill 8.39/8.24 vs 7.55. Best fleet number yet; bar = 3.54 single-box. Loopback 12L truncation: 7.33 -> 8.84 t/s. **Attribution round 2 (2026-07-14, measured on the live fleet): the EP decode is LATENCY-dominated, not compute-dominated.** Facts: .11 runs ~1.0-1.6 cores of 16 during decode with all 8 pool threads engaged evenly at ~20% duty (per-thread /proc sampling), so worker expert math is only ~2.3 ms per layer (~100-130 ms/token); the coordinator main thread is recv-blocked ~2/3 of each token (wchan=wait_woken histogram); META_TIMING shows 2 meta pieces/token and only ~250 ms/token inside the meta backend. Reconstruction: token ~= 43 per-layer boundary cycles x ~7 ms (2.3 ms expert math + ~4-5 ms turnaround: worker cmd parse, uid cache lookup, threadpool wake/join, response send, RTT) + CUDA attention (~50-80 ms) + tail. CONSEQUENCES: (a) mirrored-middle-op placement and more worker threads are near-worthless at batch 1 (workers 80-93% idle); (b) remaining single-stream levers are per-cycle turnaround polish - worker-side spin threadpool (ggml_backend_cpu_set_threadpool with polling, workers have idle cores to burn), fewer syscalls per boundary - worth maybe 10-20%; (c) the ~3-4.5 t/s single-stream ceiling estimate for this fleet+model stands; batch remains the scaling axis. Image roll 2d50d2b42 regression-free: 1.93 t/s decode fresh-cache run (noise band 1.9-2.24). **Spin-threadpool RESOLVED NEGATIVE (2026-07-14, db6b42db6, fleet-measured): GGML_RPC_THREADPOOL_POLL attaches a persistent (optionally busy-polling) pool to worker CPU backends - without it every subgraph creates+joins a disposable pool. Loopback +2% and token-identical, but fleet poll=50: 1.97/1.97 t/s vs off-band 1.93-2.24 (median 2.08) - no gain, possibly a hair of loss. Thread create/wake is NOT the per-boundary turnaround; the cycle cost lives in the message sequencing (worker serve loop, per-command round trips). Feature stays env-gated OFF (harmless, may help other worker classes). The identified NEXT single-stream lever, if ever pursued: a fused boundary command (proto 4.5-class GRAPH_COMPUTE_RETURN: worker computes a subgraph and returns the boundary tensor in the SAME response; reduced value + next subgraph accepted in one message) - halves the per-boundary messages, est. 480 -> ~400 ms/token. Deeper redesign; not started.** **BUILT AND LANDED after all (2026-07-14, 9d735e3fb + 8ba6baee1, proto 4.5): the fused boundary command.** One GRAPH_FUSED message per wire member per boundary carries the reduced value (SET), the CHAIN of subgraphs up to and including the next reduce subgraph (uid list) and the request for that boundary's partial, answered in the response - and broadcast boundaries with a local sole computer distribute through the same path, so the fetch rides across them too. Loopback plumbing collapse: GET_TENSOR 1584 -> 72 and RECOMPUTE_UID 1764 -> 252 per run. Gates: PPL fused == unfused BIT-EQUAL (8 chunks, 12L truncation); byte-identity confirmed then found FLAKY on CUDA-owner configs (the same config self-differs run-to-run - gotcha #4 routing noise observed live; PPL is the gate). Fleet paired same-day A/B: fused 1.97 vs unfused 1.81 t/s (+9%); absolute numbers sat in the historical noise band, and late-day fleet measurements gained a confound: the production MTP server was recreated onto :latest mid-session and now holds both V100s. Pipeline enters at a piece's first star boundary, never spans graph calls, auto-off under GGML_META_TIMING / NO_STAR / pre-4.5 workers; GGML_META_NO_FUSED + GGML_META_FUSED_BCAST=0/1/2 for A/B. Fleet worker images rpc-worker-9d735e3fb / 75-9d735e3fb deployed. **FINAL fused A/B on proto-4.6 workers (85ec03b1f; chains re-gated on minor>=6 after the 4.5 format changed under it - mixed fleets degrade to unfused), clean box (prod serving stopped): fused decode 2.36 / 2.56 t/s (423/390 ms/token) vs paired unfused 1.72 - +37-49%, engagement 43,344 GRAPH_FUSED calls with plain GETs collapsed 22k -> 516 per run. NEW BEST fleet single-stream: 2.56 t/s (session arc: 1.82 -> 2.08/2.24 star -> 2.56 fused), 72% of the 3.54 single-box bar; prefill 8.4 -> ~4.1-4.5 on the 10-token probe is prompt-size noise, ub-256 prefill unchanged. The boundary cycle now costs roughly one round trip per layer plus CUDA attention.** **Increment 4 MEASURED (2026-07-13): EP batching scales - the #31 revisit is confirmed.** llama-batched-bench, full V4, fleet EP config (CUDA0 owner + .11/.15, npp 64 ntg 32): TG aggregate **1.66 / 2.71 / 3.82 / 4.73 t/s at B=1/2/4/8** (x2.85 at B=8, vs the #31 pipeline law's +27% - the EP composition law holds for batching too; boundary cost is paid once per step regardless of B). Aggregate crosses the 3.54 single-stream single-box bar at B=4. Coherence gate: llama-parallel -np 4 on the full fleet model - all four concurrent temp-0 responses fluent and on-topic (identical prompts gave identical responses). Multi-sequence dsv4 cache works on the EP config unmodified - no code needed for this increment; it is a property of the meta EP design. HONEST CAVEAT: the single box ALSO scales (2x V100 -ncmoe 99, same bench: 3.85/5.37/7.07/8.35, x2.17) and stays ahead in absolute V4 throughput at every B on this hardware - V4 half-fits in 46 GB RAM with NVMe paging behind it. EP's absolute win remains the capacity regime (a model/fleet where no single box holds the experts); its batching win is the better SCALING slope plus not burning the coordinator's RAM/NVMe. Slow-link async participation (.25 batch-only/expert-paging) stays PARKED as stretch - not needed for V4 capacity and the boundary-latency law rules it out of the sync ring. **New single-box baseline (2026-07-13): V4 on the coordinator, 2x V100 `-ngl 99` + `-ncmoe 99` (all routed experts in a CPU buffer, mmap-backed - 46 GB RAM holds ~half the ~80 GB of experts, rest pages from NVMe): decode 3.09 t/s cold -> 3.54 t/s warm (127-token run, page cache saturated at 41 GB), prefill 1.6 t/s. Slightly ABOVE the 2.7-3.1 ssd-streaming baseline - the best single-box V4 number on this hardware, and the number the fleet EP path has to beat.** — **phase 0 DONE (2026-07-11): `docs/expert-parallel-plan.md`** — design + projections from the fleet's measured numbers. Core: the composition law flips from #31's SUM-of-stages to **max-over-contacted-workers** (a slow box owning few/cold experts adds capacity while ~never gating a token — the "smallest computable chunk" principle, user-guided). Wire math: 8-16 KB activations out / 16-48 KB expert outputs back per contacted worker per MoE layer; latency floor ≈ layers × RTT ≈ 13 ms/token on this LAN. **The prize: DeepSeek-V4-Flash 81 GB becomes RAM-resident across the fleet's pooled 176 GB → projected 18-40 t/s vs today's 2.7-3.1 SSD-streamed — 7-15×.** Implementation route identified: the meta tensor-split backend ALREADY does intra-layer expert segmentation (AXIS_1 gate_up + mul_mat_id PARTIAL, validated on Qwen/GLM); increment 1 = run a meta device over RPC member devices (proto 4.2 gives async+events; the per-layer reduce is a k-vector sum, NOT a GEMM row-split reduce — #17's Ethernet-AllReduce verdict doesn't apply). Increments + gates in the plan doc: (1) meta-over-RPC spike w/ 35B experts on 2 workers, (2) the DeepSeek fleet run (gate: ≥3× SSD baseline), (3) bandwidth-weighted then hot/cold placement (routing stats), (4) EP batching (where MoE -np CAN scale, unlike #31's finding for pipelines). Original filing below. The expert is the most network-friendly compute unit in the stack: fully independent FFN per expert (zero cross-expert communication), merge = a weighted add of k small vectors, demand-driven (only k of 128-256 needed per token). Design sketch: workers own expert SUBSETS (per-layer or hashed); the coordinator runs attention/router locally and ships each token's activations only to the workers owning its routed experts; workers return their expert outputs for the weighted sum. Marries the two proven tracks — ssd-streaming's per-expert machinery (slices, id→slot routing) + the RPC worker fleet — and is how large-scale MoE serving distributes. Would let N cheap RAM boxes collectively hold a model none of them fits (e.g. 81 GB DeepSeek across 2×64 GB CPU workers) with per-token traffic ~k×(2×hidden×4B) ≈ tens of KB. Key property vs pipeline: expert calls run in PARALLEL across workers — adding boxes cuts per-token time instead of adding stages. Open questions: router locality (hot experts pinned locally), per-worker batching, latency. Big effort; distributed phase 5. **INCREMENT 3 CLOSED (2026-07-15): bandwidth-weighted EP placement is now AUTOMATED — `--rpc-auto-weight` supports `-sm tensor`: shares map to the meta device's members (plain registry order, the tensor-branch device list), score-proportional and capacity-capped (compute allowance only; no whole-layer margin, KV lives with the owner), `LLAMA_META_ATTN_OWNER=<j>` gets a hard 0%% share tagged "(attention owner)"; worker-CPU members keep their scores (RAM members are the point in EP; the #35f sibling rule stays layer-only), #31 law-1 warning suppressed (EP is the capacity play). Gates (loopback): 0.6B 2-member tensor mode 41.2/58.8 exactly score-proportional, output unchanged; trunc-V4 3-member with owner=0 -> 0%%/49.7%%/50.3%% (capacity cap engaged) and temp-0 output BYTE-IDENTICAL to the single-CPU reference; -sm layer path regression-unchanged (same KV+compute reserve line, same shares). Composes with #38 Include (a reload now re-splits EP fleets automatically) — drop COORD_TS and set COORD_AUTO_WEIGHT=1 in the EP scripts to use it. Hot/cold routing-stats placement is RESOLVED BY THE LATENCY LAW (attribution round 2: boundary round trips dominate; placement cannot reduce boundary count in the sync ring — the .25 cold-share negative already showed shares do not matter for slow LINKS), leaving async slow-link participation + EP batching as the parked increment-4 stretch.** **INCREMENT 4 CLOSED (2026-07-16): batched EP SERVING is now safe - a fleet-killer in multi-slot serving found and fixed.** `llama-server -np 4` over the EP topology was never actually validated (batched-bench and the llama-parallel coherence gate ran 2026-07-13, BEFORE the fused-boundary/build-cache stack landed 07-14) and crashed the attention-owner worker within 2-3 rounds of mixed 5-concurrent-request traffic: the kv-cell ids graph input (`Meta#leaf_14`) arrived as random 64-bit garbage and set_rows aborted the worker (`ops.cpp:5063 GGML_ASSERT(i1 >= 0 && i1 < ne1)`). ROOT CAUSE: the meta prepared-build cache keys on an outer-graph content hash that mixes `t->data` - but sched-allocated meta tensors carry LOGICAL offsets, identical across two same-shaped scheduler arenas, and the buffer identity was mixed only for NON-meta tensors; when a decode-graph cache entry was EVICTED (default 4 entries < np4's ubatch-shape diversity) and a same-shaped entry rebuilt, the build cache produced a FALSE HIT whose member subgraphs still referenced the dead entry's freed worker-side allocations. Bisect (trunc-V4 6L, 3 CPU loopback workers, owner=0, 12 mixed rounds x 5 concurrent): default = crash at round 2-3 (3/3 runs) - `GGML_META_MAX_GRAPHS=128` still crashes (NOT ring age) - `LLAMA_DECODE_GRAPH_CACHE=0` survives - `=32` survives (eviction-dependent, nails it). FIX: meta buffer contexts carry a never-recycled uid, mixed into the build key for meta-buffer tensors - an evicted+rebuilt arena now misses and rebuilds (steady-state hits unaffected: a live arena's uid is stable). Gates: the exact crashing config survives 12/12 rounds; decode-graph reuse intact (605-649 graphs reused/slot); Qwen3-0.6B dense 2-worker tensor-mode temp-0 BYTE-IDENTICAL to the no-RPC reference. `set_rows` now logs dst/src1 names + index/bounds before the abort (the canary for stale graph inputs). CAVEAT recorded: pre-fix, the same stale-build reuse could corrupt np>1 tensor-mode outputs SILENTLY where the garbage landed in a non-asserting input - treat any pre-2026-07-16 multi-slot tensor-mode serving as suspect. **Async slow-link participation CLOSED NEGATIVE by physics** (the other inc-4 half): network-backed expert paging at 100-Mbit = ~12.5 MB/s payload vs the local NVMe's measured 1.6-2.7 GB/s O_DIRECT - 130-216x slower per byte, on top of the already-measured boundary-latency law (.25 cold-share: 0.42 vs 1.82 t/s) - and batch-only participation is structurally impossible without duplicating expert weights (a box that owns experts is in the ring for every token that routes to them). Remaining (not code, needs a fleet window + the fixed image): live -np 2/4/8 serving A/B to put real numbers behind the batched-bench slope; the EP compose keeps `-np 1` default until then.

    **Blocker chain, 3 of 4 fixed** (repro: worker `ggml-rpc-server -tp -p 5006x`, coordinator `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" llama-server -m /models/Qwen3-0.6B-BF16.gguf --rpc 127.0.0.1:5006x -ngl 99`):
    1. ✅ shadow keying: compute-ring entries carry an identity fingerprint (type/shape/op/params/data/view_offs/name — mutable fields like `buffer`/`flags`/src pointers deliberately excluded); mismatch = miss, recreated on demand (slice placement derives from `tensor->data`, valid for sched-allocated buffers). Static-container entries keep upstream blind pointer trust: they are only registered in-process where addresses are stable, and their per-device placement does NOT follow the recreation formula (a full-struct snapshot guard there falsely invalidated weights whose `buffer` field is assigned after registration → CUDA invalid-argument at load; fixed). All buffer/backend set/get paths route through the self-healing accessor. In-process regression gate: Qwen temp-0 byte-identical.
    2. ✅ graph derivation resolved weight leaves as MIRRORED: buffer USAGE never crossed RPC, so the worker's compute buffer read as non-COMPUTE and graph intermediates took the static-callback branch (name miss → mirrored). Fixed by implementing the upstream FIXME: optional `set_usage` hook in `ggml_backend_buffer_i` (last field, aggregate-init compatible), called from `ggml_backend_buffer_set_usage`; RPC client mirrors usage via new `RPC_CMD_BUFFER_SET_USAGE`.
    3. ✅ diagnosis tooling: `GGML_META_DEBUG=2` now also prints per-src resolution (`SRC_RESOLVE`); the mul_mat fallthrough abort names the node and src states.
    4. ✅ **TP island E2E WORKS** (Qwen3-0.6B: coherent output, 148 t/s, split weights + KV across both island GPUs). Root cause was not graph lifetime but `rpc_server::get_tensor` on results: each RPC command deserializes a fresh struct whose `src[]` are meaningless, and reading e.g. logits re-derived the split state through those srcs. Fix: shadow entries store their split state at registration plus a `by_data` identity index; `adopt_identity()` aliases identity-matched structs and seeds the split-state cache so no path ever walks deserialized srcs. Also: graph node shadows force-refreshed once per node per build (`ensure(..., force)`) since INIT_TENSOR-time registrations lack src links. In-process gate: byte-identical.
       **27B island benchmark (loopback)**: tg 33.3 t/s, coherent temp-0 output; all three push sites fire (865 weight + 64 KV + 96 recurrent-state uploads). Two more RPC fixes were needed for the hybrid arch: null-safe view_src->buffer checks (coordinator-side CPU s_copy views deserialize with null buffers) and the split-state push in `llama-memory-recurrent.cpp` (recurrent R/S cache allocates separately from the attention KV cache). References: 47.8 in-process tensor, 32.3 RPC pipeline. Follow-ups: (i) tg gap vs in-process is the synchronous per-token RPC chain (inputs SET + logits GET each round-trip; phase 2 async is the designed remedy) - note the island-as-remote-whole-model case is pessimal, the real topology only offloads a layer slab; (ii) ✅ VRAM audit done: zero mirrored fallbacks; the waste was allocation geometry - per-device buffers spanned the full linear space. Fixed: meta buft get_alloc_size returns per-device slice sizes, RPC client always queries islands for sizes, server-side sanitization treats meta tensors as logical addresses. Island VRAM 19.6 -> 12.1 GB/GPU (load path verified; final generation E2E interrupted mid-run - one smoke test pending). Smoke follow-through (2026-07-07): the interrupted run's crash (`get_tensor` bounds reject during prompt-checkpoint save) is fixed by the sanitization exemptions at HEAD, but exposed a worse silent bug: state save/restore (slot API, prompt checkpoints) reads KV via per-stream *view* structs, and view links don't survive RPC - the worker derived MIRRORED for the bare struct, reading garbage and stomping packed slices on restore. Fixed: RPC client resolves views to their root tensor with translated offset before serializing (`rpc_resolve_view`); meta set/get_tensor aborts loudly on unresolvable aliases of registered static entries instead of corrupting. Verified on 0.6B island: save byte-identical to in-process TP save, restore round-trip byte-identical generation; in-process TP gen/save/restore byte-identical to pre-change. (Note: TP saves differ from single-GPU saves by ~40% of bytes - low-mantissa allreduce noise, not layout; round-trips fine either way.) 27B island E2E at HEAD: ✅ COMPLETE - loads from worker cache in 40-50s, **9.2 GB/GPU** (slice packing landed even better than the 12.1 first measured), tg 32-33 t/s short ctx / 29.2 at 5.2k ctx, coherent output; 5212-token prefill created a real hybrid context checkpoint (149.6 MiB state_seq_get_data over RPC - the exact former crash site) cleanly; explicit slot save/restore of a 501 MB / 5259-token state round-trips with byte-identical post-restore generation. Item (ii) closed.; (iii) first-load streaming is ~40 min - worker `-c` cache makes it one-time.
       Remaining polish: (a) ✅ adopted-alias lifetime hardened (2026-07-07): ring-sourced aliases now register into the source entry's own slot so alias and shadows die in the same ggml_reset (ring lookups are fingerprint-checked, so address recycling stays safe); static-sourced aliases stay in the current ring slot — their shadows are immortal and the static container's pointer lookup skips fingerprint checks, so aliases must never live there. Verified: island save bytes unchanged, 12 shape-varied gens (> ring depth 8) + post-rotation restore round-trip byte-identical, in-process TP gen byte-identical. (b) ✅ done (see 27B island E2E above); (c) ✅ done (abort no longer derefs src names).
  - 🔄 **Phase 2 (async RPC) — infrastructure landed (proto 4.2, 2026-07-07)**: `RPC_CMD_PING` completion markers exploit strict per-connection ordering (no server state); client tracks ping sequence numbers per socket, blocking commands drain outstanding pings first; RPC backend now implements `set_tensor_async`, real `synchronize` (marker round trip), and full events (record = deferred ping, wait = drain; same-endpoint waits are free by ordering) — device caps report async+events, so llama's **pipeline parallelism gate now engages** for multi-RPC-device layer splits ("pipeline parallelism enabled", n_copies=4). Verified: 2-worker loopback pipeline (0.6B, one worker per GPU) byte-identical to single-GPU reference, ON==OFF outputs, workers clean. Measured: no throughput change at this scale (tg ~198 both; pp 1957-tok ~6100 t/s @ub2048, ~490 t/s @ub256 ON==OFF) — profiling shows the serializer is the **cross-stage activation copy**, which the sched bridges through the coordinator as a blocking `get_tensor` (stage-0 wait) before the stage-1 set, defeating ubatch overlap regardless of n_copies. Next lever: the bridged copy. A client-side "deferred GET → SET on arrival" thread cannot preserve command ordering on the destination socket (the SET must precede the next GRAPH_COMPUTE, which the client thread sends immediately), so the correct design is **worker-side pull**: new `RPC_CMD_COPY_FROM_REMOTE {endpoint, src, dst}` sent to the destination worker (send-only, ordered naturally in its stream); the worker fetches from the source worker directly. Prerequisite ✅ done (2026-07-07): rpc-server is now multi-connection — thread per connection, one shared rpc_server (buffer registry + stored graphs), an exec mutex serializing command execution (idle recv waits happen unlocked; the lock is exactly the cross-connection dependency a pull's GET needs to wait out an in-flight compute). Per-connection buffer ownership preserves the old reclaim semantics (verified: killing a coordinator frees its buffers, 1709→361 MiB, worker keeps serving); stored graphs carry an owner connection and RECOMPUTE from another connection fails loudly instead of running the wrong graph. Verified: generation byte-identical with concurrent probe connections churning. ✅ **Fenced worker-to-worker pull landed (2026-07-07)**: `GET_CONN_ID` + per-connection executed-command counters (client sent-counter kept in lockstep, HELLO included) + `GET_TENSOR_FENCED` (source side: cv wait WITHOUT the exec lock, 10-min ceiling, wakes on connection death) + `COPY_FROM_REMOTE` (dest side pulls as a client over a cached persistent peer socket; soft-fails to the bridged copy) + client `cpy_tensor_async` for RPC→RPC across endpoints, version-gated (4.2 coordinator + 4.1 worker = features off; 4.1 coordinator refuses 4.2 workers). RAW hazard = the fence; WAR hazard = the blocking COPY ack (coordinator queues no further source work until the read is out — the CUDA cross-device event contract). Kill switch `GGML_RPC_NO_W2W=1`. Bug found on the way: the client async-state registry is keyed by raw socket address, and a recycled allocation inherited a dead connection's counters — every fence hung; fixed by resetting state on socket creation. Verified (2 workers sharing GPU1, CPU coordinator, `-sm layer`): bridged and W2W outputs byte-identical to the single-GPU reference and to each other; persistent peer connection confirmed; island save/restore unaffected. Loopback perf parity as expected — the win (1 direct hop vs 2 coordinator hops per handoff) needs real two-box hardware to measure. Usage notes documented in `docs/distributed-inference-guide.md` §5. Topology note (measured 2026-07-07, 0.6B, 1 local + 1 RPC stage, ub256): upstream's remote-stages-first default order is right — forcing the local stage first via `--device` measured ~33% SLOWER prefill (4080 vs ~6000 t/s); keep the default. TCP_NODELAY: already set on both sides (transport.cpp), audit done. ✅ Fixed: coordinator now fails fast with a clear error when an RPC server is unreachable (was: silent CPU fallback). Phase 3 (cross-host NCCL) still parked unless RDMA/25GbE exists.

  - **Increment 2 (2026-07-08, `ggml-ssd-stream.{cpp,h}`): SLRU expert cache — MEASURED, NEGATIVE, keep LRU default.** Added an optional segmented LRU (probation+protected, 2nd-hit promotion, evict probation-tail-first) behind `LLAMA_SSD_STREAM_SLRU=1` (`LLAMA_SSD_STREAM_PROTECTED_PCT`, default 80). **DeepSeek 81 GB, 1×V100, `-ngl 99` decode hit rate (deterministic): 8 GB 54.0% (LRU) vs 52.9% (SLRU) · 20 GB 68.1 vs 67.8 · 30 GB 73.4 vs 73.6.** SLRU ties at 30 GB, marginally worse at 8/20 GB — routed-expert reuse isn't skewed enough for scan resistance to pay for itself; **LRU stays default (simpler, >= SLRU everywhere), SLRU kept opt-in.** Hit rates *beat* the feasibility projections (44% @ 34 GB); 30 GB → 73% hit → **3.1 t/s** (up from the 1.66 colder figure). Output-neutral (SLRU==LRU==big-budget byte-identical on the deterministic Qwen `-ngl 0` path). Also landed a `LLAMA_SSD_STREAM_NO_ODIRECT=1` buffered-read kill-switch and a hit-rate% debug line.

Confirmed streaming-specific (resident `-sm layer` = 100 t/s, no OOM). Fix: skip the streamed-expert offload when `n_gpu > 1` (keep them CPU-tier — correct, since GPU landing is single-GPU-only), removing the inflated reserve at the source; single-GPU untouched. Verified: `-sm layer` OOM lines 2→0, coherent (7.8 t/s); single-GPU PPL unchanged. Detail: `docs/ssd-streaming-plan.md §8.2.10`.
  - **Multi-GPU crash fix — DONE (2026-07-09): GPU landing is cleanly single-GPU-only.** GPU landing *engaged* under `-sm layer` (the auto-offload routes streamed experts through per-device CUDA backends) and **crashed** — `GGML_ASSERT(id >= 0 && id < n_expert)` (garbage ids from per-node orig_ids across 2 backends) + a class-only pool key aliasing a device-1 `input_cpy` onto device-0 slots. Fix: (1) pool key gained the backend → one pool per (class, device); (2) a single-GPU gate `ggml_backend_sched_n_gpu(sched)==1` guards the shrink + ssd_gpu detection + pool alloc + bind consistently, so `-sm layer` stays a clean CPU-tier fallback (and the reclaim shrink only fires where bind handles the node). Verified: `-sm layer` exit 139→0 coherent (pools=0, 3.5 t/s); single-GPU unchanged (73.69 MiB, **PPL 7.0898==baseline**); `-sm tensor` exit 0 no abort. Full multi-GPU landing left for later (2-GPU streaming has no single-stream speedup over 1-GPU landing). Detail: `docs/ssd-streaming-plan.md §8.2.9`.
  - **3.3 parallel miss-path reads — DONE (2026-07-09): prefill win, decode-neutral.** Env-gated profiling (`GGML_SSD_STREAM_DEBUG`) sized the GPU-landing miss path on DeepSeek-81GB: **read 26 s vs H2D-issue 16 s**, and **62% of VRAM misses also miss RAM** → a single-threaded O_DIRECT pread (~1.5 GB/s) dominated. Parallelized the preads (`LLAMA_SSD_STREAM_READ_THREADS`, default 1 = unchanged): `gpu_bind` now (1) touches pool + reserves RAM slot serially (`ssd_touch` gained a `defer_read` mode — SLRU isn't thread-safe), (2) preads RAM-missed slices across N workers each with its own O_DIRECT bounce buffer, (3) H2Ds every miss. **Result (96 tok, sweep 1/2/4/8): read 25.9→20.1 s (−22%, saturates at 2); prefill +31% (1.3→1.7 t/s); decode neutral (2.5→2.6 t/s, noise)** — decode touches ~6 experts/layer so within-call parallelism has little to chew on, and the token is co-bound by the untouched pageable H2D. Correctness: DeepSeek PPL rt=1 4.5240 == rt=4 4.5240. Verdict: helps the batch-amortized path (prefill/long prompts), not single-stream decode of a model this far over VRAM; kept env-gated. Further decode gains need H2D overlap+pin (Volta side-stream, risky per task 17) — not pursued. Detail: `docs/ssd-streaming-plan.md §8.2.8`.
  - **3.2e-a input_cpy VRAM reclaim — DONE (2026-07-09).** Reclaimed the dead-weight VRAM gallocr reserves for each streamed-expert `MUL_MAT_ID` input copy: `gpu_bind` redirects `input_cpy->data` to the persistent slot pool, so the full n_expert-slice copy is never read. New `ggml_ssd_stream_gpu_shrink_copy()` shrinks the copy to 1 slice at the copy-creation site in `ggml_backend_sched_split_graph` before gallocr sizes the arena; compute is unchanged (bind already overwrote ne[2]/nb[2]/data; residual nb[3] unused since ne[3]=1). **Correctness gate: PPL identical** (Qwen streamed+landing, 16 chunks: ON 7.0898 == OFF 7.0898; greedy temp-0 text is not usable — `-ngl 99` GPU-MoE near-tie noise flips output independent of the flag). **Reclaim (CUDA0 compute buffer): Qwen 407.00→73.69 MiB (−82%), DeepSeek-81GB 1344.25→517.77 MiB (−61%, ~826 MiB)** — additive cache headroom, removes the exit=137 pressure; hit/t·s identical at equal budget. Safety: shrink does NOT allocate the pool (runs pre-load when the registry is empty → would mis-size pools by class count, a bug I hit and fixed: 4×6.29 GB instead of 1.57 GB); pool allocated on the compute path, fallback aborts loudly if a shrunk copy is ever unhandled (catastrophic-OOM only). Kill-switch `LLAMA_SSD_STREAM_GPU_NO_RECLAIM=1`. Detail: `docs/ssd-streaming-plan.md §8.2.7`.

The `-cmoe` control (experts on a NORMAL CPU buffer, same partial offload) scored correct 6.20, which + reading the scheduler (antirez/ds4 study pointed at all-position being special) located it. **Fix: `ggml_ssd_stream_prefill_experts()` hook called from that block to make the used experts resident before the copy; no-op when streaming off.** Verified: Qwen-35B-A3B streamed PPL 158 -> **6.2022, byte-identical to `-cmoe` and resident**; flagship **DeepSeek-V4-Flash 81GB on ONE V100 -> sane PPL 4.0568** (was 100+ garbage). Same bug also caused the generation near-tie flip (first-use reads unfilled arena); that flip persists at `-ngl 0` SERIAL (5541 vs 7e35) but is now **proven quality-neutral** (PPL identical). Caveat: large prefill ubatches need budget >= one ubatch's expert union. `-np>1` concurrent streamed serving verified (2-slot server, 2 concurrent temp-0 requests coherent). Detail in `docs/ssd-streaming-plan.md §8` item 2.
  - **UX / CLI semantics (to finalize in design):** three combinable tiers — resident-GPU (`-ngl N`), resident-CPU (today's host layers), and streamed-SSD (new). `--ssd-streaming` alone = stream everything (min-memory mode); `-ngl N --ssd-streaming` = N hot layers resident in VRAM, rest streamed; all three = resident GPU slab + resident CPU slab + streamed tail. Likely knobs: `--ssd-stream-budget <MiB>` (pinned staging-ring size), placement policy (which layers get which tier — front-loaded resident vs access-frequency), and a per-tensor override story consistent with `-ot`/`--override-tensor`.
  - **Physics / honest expectations (measured on this box 2026-07-07):** the models NVMe sustains **1.6 GB/s** O_DIRECT sequential reads (`dd iflag=direct`, 4 GiB from a resident GGUF). A dense 27B Q4 (16.4 GB) fully streamed touches all bytes every token → **~0.1 t/s decode ceiling**; this is a *runnability* feature, not a speed feature, for dense decode. The economically interesting cases: (a) **prefill/batch amortization** — one layer stream serves the whole ubatch, so a 4096-token prefill pays the 16 GB stream once (~10 s) across thousands of tokens; (b) **MoE expert streaming** — only route-active experts per token, plus a hot-expert LRU cache in RAM/VRAM (GLM-class A3B: ~3B active of ~30B total → ~10x fewer bytes/token than dense); (c) **mostly-resident mixes** — e.g. 2 streamed layers out of 48 adds only ~0.4 GB/token ≈ +0.25 s/token.
  - ✅ **Phase 0 done (2026-07-07)** — `docs/ssd-streaming-plan.md`: measured baselines (CPU-resident 1.51 t/s vs mmap-thrash 0.21 t/s under a 14 GB cgroup cap with cold cache — the number to beat; caps without page-cache eviction are invalid, fadvise DONTNEED first), prior-art survey (upstream has no managed streaming per discussion #19163; issue #20757's expert-cache PoC = 12-14 t/s GPT-OSS-120B on 8 GB GPU validates the MoE sweet spot), and full code map: the tree already ships `--direct-io` O_DIRECT aligned reads, per-tensor file offsets (`weights_map`), an async pinned-staging upload pipeline, the RPC buffer as the non-resident-buft template, and the decode-graph cache for per-graph prefetch schedules. Gap: `-ot` only offers device-default bufts.
  - ✅ **Phase 1 done — NEGATIVE RESULT (2026-07-07)**: mmap residency director implemented (`common/ssd-streaming.cpp`, `LLAMA_SSD_STREAMING=1`, `LLAMA_SSD_STREAM_BUDGET=<MiB>`; byte-identical by construction, verified). 27B @ 14 GB cap, cold cache: thrash 0.266 t/s vs director 0.274 t/s — **no win**. Dense access is sequential and kernel readahead already overlaps IO with compute near-optimally (~3.7 s/token vs ~10 s serial floor); the disk is bandwidth-saturated, steering can't add bandwidth. Director stays as env-gated scaffolding (its ring/cursor/callback machinery is what phase 4 needs). **Redirect: phase 4 (MoE expert caching — the one access pattern the kernel can't predict) promoted to next; phase 2 (GPU tier) unaffected by the null result.**
  - **Phase 4 feasibility measured (same day)**: GLM MoE CPU 10.87 t/s uncapped → **1.12 t/s @ 10 GB cap** (10x collapse = the prize is real) — but per-selection `madvise(WILLNEED)` steering made it **60x worse** (0.018 t/s): WILLNEED blocks in direct reclaim inside a cgroup at its limit, ~1,100 calls/token. Page-cache hinting is dead for this tier in BOTH forms (dense: no win; MoE: catastrophic). **The viable phase-4/2 design is a userspace expert cache — pread/O_DIRECT into an owned (pinned) arena with explicit SLRU — which is the same machinery for CPU and GPU tiers; they merge into one build.** All measured evidence + the steering code preserved (env-gated) in common/ssd-streaming.cpp and the plan doc.
  - **Phase 2/4 feasibility spike — MEASURED PASS, GO (2026-07-08):** the design's one make-or-break number is random small-block O_DIRECT read bandwidth (the cache-miss path). Measured on this NVMe against the real 81 GB `DeepSeek-V4-Flash-IQ2XXS` (`scripts/ssd-stream-bench-odirect.cpp`): random 2.16 MB O_DIRECT reads = 1672 MB/s (1 thread) → **~2700 MB/s (2+ threads)**; 7.08 MB sets = 2448 → **~2800 MB/s**. Random reads *exceed* the 1.6 GB/s sequential baseline and O_DIRECT never touches the page cache (so the WILLNEED direct-reclaim −60x trap cannot recur). Model layout (`scripts/ssd-stream-gguf-layout.py`): deepseek4, 43 layers × 256 experts, 6 used + 1 shared/token; **77.9 GB of 81 GB is experts** (gate/up IQ2_XXS ~2.16 MB/slice, down Q2_K ~2.75 MB/slice), non-expert only **8.8 GB** (fits RAM or 1 GPU). Cold expert traffic = 258 slice-sets × 7.08 MB = **1.83 GB/token**. Projected IO-bound decode @2.7 GB/s: 0% cache **1.48 t/s** (already beats the 1.12 mmap-thrash floor by using pread not faults) · 44% (34 GB RAM cache) **2.64** · 80% skew **7.39** · 95% **29.6** (→ compute-bound). **Verdict: build the userspace O_DIRECT pread + SLRU expert cache.** The 81 GB DeepSeek (fits neither VRAM nor RAM) is the reference vehicle. Next: implement the streamed buffer type (below), then measure real hit rate + compute-bound ceiling.
  - **Design sketch (to validate in phase 0):** a *streamed* backend buffer type whose tensors are backed by `(file, offset)` instead of resident memory; a pinned host staging ring sized by `--ssd-stream-budget`; prefetch driven by graph execution order — double-buffer layer n+1's tensors while layer n computes (the graph-plan/scheduler hook point is the same place the decode-graph cache lives, so per-layer prefetch lists can be precomputed per cached graph shape); reads via O_DIRECT (+ io_uring) so streaming does not evict the resident CPU tier from page cache; no eviction complexity (weights are immutable, read-only); dequant stays on GPU exactly as today. GGUF is already offset-addressable per tensor — no file-format work needed.
  - **Phased plan:** 0 = investigation + baselines + plan doc (above); 1 = CPU-tier managed streaming (prefetch thread, staging ring), gate: byte-identical output + beats mmap-thrash tg measurably; 2 = GPU-tier streaming (staging ring → async H2D on a side stream overlapped with layer compute); 3 = placement policy + CLI + docs + compose profile; 4 = MoE-aware expert streaming with hot-expert cache; 5 (stretch) = cuFile/GDS direct SSD→VRAM, and interplay with distributed (task 12): worker-side streaming would let a TP island serve models bigger than the island's combined VRAM.
  - **Verification (house rules):** temp-0 byte-exactness vs fully-resident runs at every phase; enforced ceilings (RSS + VRAM measured under load); a tg/pp table per tier mix in docs/perf-tuning-v100.md.
- [x] **16. Meta shadow-arena abort on large graphs** — ✅ fixed (`4c837a751`, 2026-07-08). Production crash: `GGML_ASSERT(obj_new)` / "not enough space in the context's memory pool", 100% reproducible with two requests on Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (MTP, slot reuse) and at the end of long prefills on the 27B. Root cause: compute-ring shadow containers size their arenas as `compute_headroom` (16) views per static tensor; DeltaNet/hybrid graphs create more recurrent-state views per graph than that (27B: 64 layers → 2048 slots, 35B: 40 → 1280 — exactly the crashing pool sizes; both overflowed by exactly one `ggml_tensor_overhead()`). Fix at the mechanism: arenas grow on demand (full arena retired with its shadows alive, fresh same-size arena chained; ring rotation frees the chain) instead of a bigger constant that the next model shape would hit. Verified: the exact 35B two-request repro completes (+ a third reuse, zero warnings); full 27B in-process MTP gate byte-identical (63.6 t/s). Every image up to `v100-bigbro` carries the bug; fixed from `v100-bigbro2`. Related latent (not this crash, noted for upstream): `ggml-backend.cpp:1774` sizes the sched context with `sizeof(struct ggml_tensor)` instead of `ggml_tensor_overhead()`, and the `×2` per-input factor understates `n_copies>1` pipeline configs.
- [x] **17. Overlap AllReduce with the next subgraph's independent prefix** — ❌ **NEGATIVE RESULT (2026-07-08): implemented, correct, measured a LOSS — reverted. Do not re-attempt graph-level reduce/compute overlap.** Motivation held up: step-0 timing (`GGML_META_TIMING=1`, landed `6a5d2920d`/`bb1b7f2c1`) measured the tax at compute 60.3 ms + reduce 9.9 ms per 27B decode graph over 128 boundaries (~77 µs/boundary, ~14-15% of step wall). Full implementation was built and gauntlet-tested (`GGML_META_OVERLAP=1`: side backend instance + event pair per device; planning-time detection of each post-reduce subgraph's independent leading prefix via view-root aliasing; prefix submitted on the side stream behind ev_main while the AllReduce runs on the main stream, main re-joins via ev_side; per-device positional `ggml_graph_view` split — sound because device subgraphs are built 1:1 with meta node indices, ggml-backend-meta.cpp:2480).
  **Why it fails, both regimes (measured 2026-07-08, 2×V100, idle GPUs, temp-0 256-tok, `GGML_CUDA_ALLREDUCE=p2p`):**
  - **Dense/hybrid (Qwen 27B): the premise is false.** Diagnostic sweep: ALL 384 reduce boundaries have the reduced tensor consumed by the IMMEDIATELY next node (`attn_output→attn_residual` ADD, `ffn_out→l_out` ADD, `linear_attn_out→its own reshape`) — the residual stream makes everything downstream transitively dependent on every reduce. Zero hoistable prefix exists; the machinery is inert. 49.2 vs 49.3 t/s (noise), byte-identical.
  - **MoE (GLM 23B): prefixes exist but overlap LOSES 47%.** 138 boundaries/graph carry a real 4-node independent prefix (next layer's `ffn_gate` MUL_MAT is independent of the previous expert-sum reduce) — path fires, output byte-identical (twice), but tg **83.0 → 44.1 t/s**: +10.6 ms/token ≈ 77 µs/boundary of event-record/wait + doubled launch-call orchestration on Volta (no CUDA graphs → every extra launch/event is real CPU cost), which exceeds the entire reduce being hidden while a 4/71-node prefix hides almost nothing.
  **Conclusion:** the ~10 ms/token reduce tax is real but unrecoverable by scheduler-level reordering — where the graph is serial there is nothing to overlap, and where something exists it is too small to pay for the synchronization. The only viable successor is fusing the reduce into the producing GEMM (Megatron/TE-style chunked mul_mat + reduce-scatter-per-chunk epilogue) — a CUDA-kernel project in the mul_mat path, high effort, uncertain on Volta. Code reverted from the tree (this record + the git log of this entry preserve the design); `GGML_META_TIMING` diagnostic remains landed.
- [x] **18. MMVQ kernel tuning for Volta (sm70)** — ✅ **PARTIAL WIN landed (2026-07-08): +1.8% batch-1 K-quant decode, ppl-identical. Prod (MTP spec) neutral.** Root cause confirmed: Volta (cc 7.0) fell through to `MMVQ_PARAMETERS_GENERIC` in `mmvq.cu`, which lumps `ncols_dst` 1-4 at nwarps=4 — untuned for sm70. Added a dedicated `MMVQ_PARAMETERS_VOLTA` table_id (device + host `get_device_table_id`, `calc_nwarps`, `calc_rows_per_block`), differing from GENERIC only at the batch-1 decode path (`ncols_dst==1`): K-quants (Q2_K…Q6_K) now use **nwarps=2** (mirrors TURING, the closest tuned arch). **Measured sweep (single-GPU tg128, Qwen 27B Q4_K):** nwarps 1=30.21, **2=31.62**, 4(old)=31.06, 8=27.75 → 2 wins, **+1.8%**. **Correctness:** nwarps changes the cross-warp dot-product reduction order (not byte-exact by construction), gated on ppl instead — **PPL 5.9063 ± 0.14168 identical to 4 decimals** vs baseline (40 wikitext chunks), output coherent. Pascal/older keep GENERIC (unaffected). **Prod impact caveat:** the flagship MTP-spec config is NEUTRAL (70.42 vs 70.41 t/s) because MTP verify decodes at batch-4 (`ncols_dst=4`), which this change doesn't touch; the win applies to batch-1 nospec decode (single-stream, `docker-compose.nospec.yml`, low-acceptance long-context tail). **Investigated but NOT shipped:** tuning `ncols_dst` 2-4 (nwarps 1/2) gave +3-4% on *pure* single-GPU batched decode (llama-batched-bench npl=4) but was prod-NEUTRAL/-0.6% on the real 2-GPU MTP-spec path (AllReduce + draft overhead dominate, not the half-width matvec) and perturbed verify numerics (draft counters 1874→1909) — reverted. Follow-up: revisit `ncols_dst` 2-4 specifically for the nospec `-np>1` aggregate profile (89.6 t/s baseline) where the small-batch matvec is a larger fraction. Harness: `llama-bench`/`llama-batched-bench`, `scratchpad/spec_bench.py`. Task 19 (FA depth decay) is the remaining same-class lever.
- [x] **19. Flash-attention long-context decay on Volta** — ✅ **CLOSED (2026-07-08, `723cf9fed`): SIZED + bottleneck identified; easy levers exhausted; deep kernel work deferred.** Prize measured (single-GPU tg, Qwen 27B, `llama-bench -d`): 31.5 (d0) → 26.2 (d32k) → 22.1 (d65k) t/s = **−30%**, i.e. ~13.5 ms/token of FA at 65k (only the full-attention layers decay; DeltaNet is depth-independent). **Decode kernel confirmed** (instrumented `ggml_cuda_get_best_fattn_kernel`): `BEST_FATTN_KERNEL_VEC` = `fattn-vec.cuh`, D=256, GQA≤2, stream-K KV-split already engaged on Volta (`fattn-common.cuh:1126` — `tiles_efficiency_percent < 75` true at batch-1). **Bottleneck class = COMPUTE/instruction-bound, NOT memory-bound** — the decisive test: quantizing KV *worsens* the depth penalty instead of halving it (d0→d65k decay: f16 −29.8%, q8_0 −32.0%, q4_0 −34.6%; Δms/tok @65k: f16 13.5, q8_0 15.2, q4_0 17.3). **→ Actionable negative: do NOT use quantized KV for long-context *speed* on Volta (it adds dequant compute to an already compute-bound kernel; quant KV is still valid purely for *capacity*).** **Easy launch-config lever exhausted:** the vec kernel hardcodes `nthreads=128` for all archs (`fattn-vec.cuh:4-11`); swept for Volta → d65k: 64=21.8, **128=22.1 (best)**, 256=21.4 — already optimal, no win (unlike task 18's nwavps knob). Remaining headroom needs genuine sm70 kernel-algorithm work (KQ dot-product instruction scheduling / dp4a use / online-softmax overhead in `fattn-vec.cuh` + `fattn-common.cuh` vec_dot) — the "high effort/risk" path, uncertain payoff, deferred to a focused kernel session. ncu profiling blocked in-container (`ERR_NVGPUCTRPERM`; would need host `NVreg_RestrictProfilingToAdminUsers=0`).
- [x] **20. Adaptive speculative draft cap** — ✅ **MEASURED (2026-07-08): tg-NEUTRAL, does not win. Keep OFF (default), do not enable in prod.** Mechanism (landed `64788b8a8`, env-gated `LLAMA_SPEC_ADAPTIVE=1`): acceptance EMA (warm-started at n_max) caps each draft round at EMA+1 within [n_min, n_max] across all three draft loops — saves draft passes on cold content; verify batch shape unaffected (drafts pad to n_max), target temp-0 output unchanged by construction (sampling policy only). **Bench (27B MTP, `-sm tensor -ts .5,.5 -np 1 -c 16384`, p-min 0.75 n-max 3, 8 mixed prompts × 256 tok, idle 2×V100):** adaptive deterministically cut draft tokens generated 1874 → 1779 (**−5%**) but aggregate tg **70.4 → 70.6 t/s (+0.3%, inside the ~0.1 t/s run-to-run noise)** and accepted tokens dropped 1303 → 1284. Confirms the honest expectation: the p_min confidence gate already captures the early-exit value, and MTP drafting is cheap relative to verify, so cutting draft passes doesn't move tg. Code stays landed (env-gated off) as a documented no-op; the `LLAMA_SPEC_ADAPTIVE=1` line added to `docker-compose.mtp.yml` was reverted (no benefit). Harness: `scratchpad/spec_bench.py` (server `draft_n`/`draft_n_accepted` timings). Next: kernel-level levers (tasks 18/19) are the only remaining tg headroom.
